TOOLING (workspace tools/, all run in WSL; see tools/*.sh header lines)
- tools/qchk.sh <list>: chk without the slow src/ rsync (2 s instead of 10 s per call).
- tools/ad.sh <file.c> <fn> <rom> <n> [xflags] [-v]  (tools/ad.py): ALIGNED diff of our @SN output vs the ROM at mnemonic level:
  prints each structural hunk (inserted/deleted insns), the number of operand-different aligned insns and register-substitution
  votes (ours->rom).  chk's "N words differ" is useless for big functions (one extra insn shifts everything); drive the work by
  "structural diff insns" first, then register votes.  Use <n> = real ROM insn count (targets.txt may count trailing junk words).
- tools/vf.py <fn> <rom> <n> - file1 file2 ...: structural + full metric for many full-file variants in one call (4 parallel compiles);
  tools/vrun.py: @@{ a @@| b @@} cartesian expansion of one template (VM=struct for structural metric).
- tools/sd.sh: side-by-side ours|ROM with '#' on differing words.  tools/dump.sh: cc1_281 with -dg -dl -dS -dR -dL -dd -dj -ds -dt dumps.
- permuter for @SN: tools/psn_sync.sh <fn> <wip/c/x.c> "<xflags>" then run permuter.py ~/perm/s704_<fn> (tools/permcc_sn.sh).
  For a 1-5 word residue it hits in minutes (func_0029E510: 5 -> 0 in 4 minutes); for >= 40 differing aligned lines it never converged.
- asm/ is not present in a fresh ws (asmfn.py prints nothing): copy asm/ from another ws; tools/asm2.py also prints alabel functions.

SOURCE-SHAPE LESSONS
- func_0029E510 (4x4 Gauss-Jordan inverse, 228 insns) went 94 -> 0 structural diffs with these steps (generally useful):
  * REUSE FEW INDEX VARIABLES: the original had only i, j, k (+ t, r): the pivot index `best` is the same variable as the inner loop
    counter j (j = k; ... j = i;).  Extra named variables (best, r as search index) change cse copy canonicalisation and loop-iv layout.
  * Hoist row indices into locals: `t = perm[k]; ... aug[t][j]`, `r = perm[i]; ... aug[r][j]` BEFORE the inner j loop.  Reading
    perm[i] inside the loop is never hoisted by gcc (the float store to aug[] may alias the int load), the ROM hoists it.
  * An input matrix indexed as `*(float *)((char *)src + (i * 16 + j * 4))` gives the ROM's `addu v0,a1,t1` with t1 = offset giv
    (init i*16, += 4); `src[i][j]` on `float (*)[4]` or `((float *)src)[i*4+j]` give a pointer giv / no giv.  Same for the dst writer.
  * abs value as `w = x; if (!(0.0f <= w)) big = -w; else big = w;` (if/else on a copy) reproduces `lwc1 f0 ; mov.s f2,f0 ; c.le.s ; bc1fl neg`.
  * The 1-word residue `neg.s f0,f1` vs ROM `neg.s f0,f0` (cse2 propagated the raw load into the negation) was cured by a DEAD
    store `w = 0.0f;` placed before the `return 1` exit (extends last_uid of w; permuter found the same effect with
    `if (w) return 1; else return 1;`).  piv/big are ONE float variable (`big = aug[t][k]; if (big == 0.0f) ...; x / big`).

MORE LESSONS (s704, @SN)
- func_00288908 (level builder, 348 insns; now EXACT in wip/out with 2 register pins; the lessons below got it from 47 structural hunks to ~6, the pins fixed the last s4/s5/s6 permutation):
  * `j = 0` at the top of the OUTER loop body must be `j = zero;` with a function-level `s32 zero = 0;` (set once, outside the loop):
    cse1 then cannot fold the inner-loop entry test (ROM: `addu s7,zero,zero ; slt v0,lastA,s7`, we got `bltz lastA`); reload later
    substitutes the constant (`addu s7,$0,$0`).  A literal 0 is folded by cse because the set is in the same EBB as the compare.
  * A spilled running offset `row += cntA` in the ROM is NOT a source variable: it is the loop.c giv of `ci * cntA` (initialised after
    the loop guard, `sw zero,row` right after the `bltz` of the guard).  Write `j + ci * cntA` at the use sites and delete `row`.
  * `off2 = nBytesB + 7; off2 = off1 + (off2 & ~7);` (in-place on one variable, found by the permuter, 3135 -> 775 score) and
    `off2 = X; off1 = off2; ... off2 = ...off1...` reproduce the ROM's `addu s6,s0,zero` copy.
- func_0029AE80: `s8 flag = (m->e == 1)` (NARROW variable type) makes every `if (flag)` compile to `addu v0,s3,zero ; beqz v0` like the ROM
  (promoted-subreg copy cse cannot propagate), `s32 c` must stay int (lh).  Table index as `*(tbl + (bit / 8 + (s16)ch * 4))`.
  The `addu v1,s0,zero` copy of the `bit` inline argument was NOT reproduced (function-level temps/`b = c`/macros/narrow formals all fail or
  make the temp callee-saved).
- cse1 canonical-register rule (copy `p = w` is kept only if p outlives w and the cse block): explains why copies of user variables
  vanish in inline expansions; `-fcse-skip-blocks` makes `if (c) x = y;` skip-blocks part of the same EBB (so values flow across them).
- Reload register for a spilled pseudo is t2 ($10) in 278530; our struct member `L.ret` loads into v0 (not a spill).  A `register s32 rt asm("$10")`
  pin turns `0` arguments in the else arm into `rt` (cse knows rt == 0 there) - not usable.
- Pinning a loop-walked pointer (`register M5Grid *gp asm("$20")`) fixes a callee-saved swap but disables its strength reduction.
- Tools: tools/prio.py (allocation order/priorities from dump.sh output), tools/fsweep.sh (single cc1 flag sweep), tools/mktgt.py +
  tools/psn2.sh (permuter target for alabel functions such as func_002A2270 / func_0029AE80; psn.sh silently makes an EMPTY target).
- func_00278530: the permuter's only useful find was REMOVING a variable (`cols = X; ... cp = cols;` -> use `cp` directly): 98 -> 65 words,
  whole structure then matches.  Merge locals that are copies of each other before reaching for pins.  After that the remaining diffs are
  allocation-priority (dst vs the walked gp pointer: gp 5 refs/len 34 = .294 beats dst 8 refs/len 92 = .261; priority = floor_log2(refs)*refs/len,
  hoisting gp's set or adding refs to dst did not flip it) and the clamp-loop temps.

  spec = {"base": file, "func":..., "rom":"0x..", "flags":"@SN", "vars": [[["old","new"],...], ...]}.

CODEGEN LESSONS
- Register pin fixes a pure allocation-priority swap without touching scheduling (func_00406178): the natural no-copy form has exactly the ROM
  schedule but gives st=s1,own=s2 (st has fewer live insns, so higher global-alloc priority); `register s32 st __asm__("$18");` makes it EXACT.
  The older draft that copied the params (own = own0 ...) fixed the allocation but changed prologue scheduling (3 words) - prefer the pin.
- Struct-return temp slots (func_00403658): a block-local 12-byte struct that is declared AFTER a struct-returning call reuses that call's freed
  temp slot (same size); declaring it as a 16-byte struct (`struct { Vec3 q; s32 pad; } v4;`) puts it in a fresh slot (0x60) while the second
  call's temp reuses the first slot (0x50) - exactly the ROM frame.  (found by s605, confirmed)
- Macro-expanded gSPScisTextureRectangle (func_002AA724): the ROM tests `(yl) < 0` on the RAW s32 register but sign-extends yl everywhere else:
  declare yl's variable s32 (x0,x1,y1 stay s16) and give the macro `(s16)(yl)` casts in MAX(yl,0) and in the product yl*dtdy, plain `(yl) < 0`.
  The dsdx/dtdy `<0` tests are on `(s16)` values.
- `pp = p->f; <asm volatile or sink> plr = pp;` does NOT keep a separate temp for a callee-saved pointer: regmove/combine rename the load straight
  into the s-reg (func_00406F7C still open).

MORE CODEGEN LESSONS (all verified EXACT: func_0040D350, func_002AA724, func_00406F7C, func_00406178)
- func_0040D350 (525 insns) matched with plain natural C after four source-shape insights (no pins):
  * an array base kept in a LOCAL pointer (`tbl = D_80153820; e = &tbl[D_80153800]`) gives the hoisted `lui/addiu t1` base that the ROM
    shares between the entry address and the search loop; indexing `D_80153820[i]` directly gives `lui at; addu at,at,a0` per field.
  * a search loop that ends in `found = 1` is an inlined helper with early `return 1;` and final `return 0;`:
    `static inline s32 O02Overlap(O02Ent *e, O02Ent *tbl)` whose for-condition reads the GLOBAL count (`i < D_80153800`, not a parameter):
    loop.c then hoists the global into a fresh pseudo (`lw v0; blez v0; ...; move a3,v0`).  Pass `tbl[i].f` (index form) not `p++`.
  * `if (a != x) changed = 1; else if (b != y) changed = 1; ... else changed = (d != w);` (if/else-if chain with `changed = 0` before)
    gives `bne ; li v0,1` slots for every arm, the ternary chain `a ? 1 : b ? 1 : ..` gave `bnel` for the third.
  * FP in-place accumulate for cross-jumped tails: `fy = (f32)base; fy -= term;` in each switch arm (the three `sub.s f20,f20,f0` merge).
  * a record accessed as `font->e[g].d/.a/.b` with `Font = { s8 pad[4]; { s32 w; s8 a,b,c,d; } e[]; }` and `gl = &font->e[g]` computed once
    gives base+4 / field offsets 4,5,7 (the ROM shape); a flat `{s8 pad[8]; {s8 a..h} e[]}` gives base+0 / 11,8,9.
  * a stray store (`it->f16 = 0` in the old draft) can hide a different problem: the ROM has NO such store; with it removed the `f32 fc = fx;`
    snapshot of the loop-carried float for the compare (`mov.s f1,f21; c.lt.s f1,f3`) matched.
- func_002AA724: the 4th coordinate pinned to its ROM register (`register s16 y1 __asm__("$16")`) fixed the whole FP/GPR permutation at once; the pinned
  variable then needs a later use (`__asm__("" : : "r"(y1))`) or gcc reuses its register as the shift temporary (`sll s0,s0,16; sra v0,s0,16`).
- func_00406F7C: a pinned temp for a pointer that is only COPIED into a callee-saved variable at the start of one arm
  (`register T *pp __asm__("$2"); pp = o->f; asm volatile(""); if (c) {...} else { plr = pp; asm volatile(""); ...}`) is the only way found to
  keep ROM's `lw v0 ... beqz ; move s0,v0` (regmove otherwise renames the load into s0).  Pinning the two address temps of a later
  `*(u32*)(A + B + off) = call()` (`register u8 *bb asm("$5"); register s32 oo asm("$3")`, `oo += (s32)bb`) fixed the last operand-order/regs words.
- Spill-slot offsets follow the order of pseudo creation: declaring the (spilled) locals in the ROM's slot order (payload, payload2, meta, fmt,
  r, saved, thr for func_00410FD4) reproduces sp+0x1DC.. exactly.  Global-alloc priority = refs*floor_log2(refs)/live_length: to flip s0/s1 between
  a temp and a variable, lengthen the live range of the one that must lose (place `saved = s` copy AFTER the following statements): func_00410FD4.
- A source statement that the ROM does not contain can still be needed by an old draft; always compare the number of stores to each struct field.
- tools/gm.py (greedy mutation search: move line / asm barrier / do-while / register pin on declarations) solved func_002AA724's last 12 words in 8 s;
  tools/hc.py = statement-order hill climb.  Both use the exact aligned-diff metric (tools/score.py) instead of the permuter score, which
  under-weights stack offsets (a permuter 'score 10' for func_00403658 was a different frame layout).

LATER LESSONS (func_0040FC14, 394 insns, EXACT with @SN from a 26-word/45-operand near-miss)
- Declaring the hot globals as members of one struct (`extern struct { ... } D_80153C0C/C20/C40`) instead of separate externs/array parameters
  changes load/store scheduling and cse enormously (struct diff 52 -> 6); do this before anything else when a pool-like set of globals is reused.
- Callee-saved allocation = global-alloc priority (refs weighted by loop depth * floor_log2 / live_length).  A pointer that is both cse'd and used 20x
  (`state = &D_801539B0...`) outranks a strength-reduced giv (stride 0x49C): cut ITS reference count by reading a field through another
  live pointer (`*(s16 *)((u8 *)recordData - 0x10)` instead of `state->f25C`, `*(s16 *)((u8 *)var_a1 - 4)` instead of `header->objects.unk0`);
  ROM does exactly this (`lh v0,-16(s3)`, `lh v0,-4(a1)`).  This swapped s4/s5 without any register pin; a pin on `state` DOES work for the
  allocation but a hard-reg base loses the alias info and the scheduler stops moving loads above stores (do not pin pointers to globals).
- Do not share one variable between a short loop temp (shift offset) and a long-lived running counter: sharing raises the counter's priority
  (it got s4, ROM s6) and also forces the temp into a callee-saved register.  Separate variables -> temp lands in a0 (local-alloc) as in ROM.
- Commutative `addu` operand/destination order (`addu a0,a0,v1` vs `addu v1,v1,a0`) follows the order the operands were evaluated: write
  `p = (void **)((i << 2) + (s32)base)` (index first) instead of `&base[i]` to get dest == the shifted index.
- hc.py (statement-order hill climb, ~5 min for 11 lines) fixed the preheader order of two strength-reduction inits (`addiu s1,s1,18` vs `move s2,s0`)
  by moving the biv increment `var_s1++` after `var_s2 += 1`: loop.c emits giv inits in biv-list order, so biv increment order = init order.
- `x = call(); st->f = x; ...; use(x)` stores x from the call-return register only if the later uses go through a DIFFERENT variable:
  `x = call(); y = x; st->f = x; ...; use(y)` gives `move s1,v0 ... sw v0,off(s5)` (store in the jal delay slot reads v0); using x everywhere gives `sw s1`.

func_00410FD4 (PARTIAL, 1 swap left): lessons that did transfer
- `extern s32 D_801539B0;` (scalar) -> `lui at; sw zero,0(at)` per access; ROM has `lui s0; addiu s0,s0,lo; lw a0,0(s0) ... sw zero,0(s0)`: declare the global as an ARRAY
  (`extern s32 D_801539B0[]`, use `[0]`) to get one materialised address register for several accesses in a block.
- To keep `addiu v0,s0,-544 ; lw v0,592(v0)` (cse folds `(g-0x88)+0x250` to `48(s0)` otherwise, even through a named pointer variable) make the base
  pointer opaque: `register u8 *st __asm__("$2"); st = (u8 *)(g - 0x88); __asm__("" : "+r"(st));` (pin alone or asm alone does not work, both together do).
- decomp-permuter (not the greedy tools) found the remaining scheduling of the 13-argument call's stack stores: the winning mutation was only
  `t = (sizes[i] = o2->rec[i].c * o2->rec[i].h);` in an earlier loop (chain assignment instead of `t = ..; sizes[i] = t;`) - it changes pseudo creation order, which
  shifts sched1 tie-breaks 150 insns later.  Always let the permuter try after the source is within ~10 hunks; its score 160 here = 3 differing words.
- SOLVED (func_00410FD4 now EXACT): the asm+pin trick above produced the right insns but perturbed sched2 (`move a2,s2` landed after the addiu).  The clean way to stop cse
  folding `(g-0x88)+0x250` into `48(s0)` is a VOLATILE read through a plain local pointer: `u8 *st = (u8 *)(g - 0x88); ... *(volatile s32 *)(st + 0x250) ...`
  -> `addiu v0,s0,-544 ; lw v0,592(v0)` and the ROM's schedule, no pin, no asm.  Try `volatile` on the access before any asm/pin when cse folds a reg+const address.
--- s706 ---
FINDINGS s706 (round 7, hard leftovers, @SN toolchain; overlay A 0x18A680..0x1B22EC)

STATUS NOTE: of the 28 targets, 20 had already been solved by earlier workspaces (s508/s607/s411/o03/o04/o05/q03/q04); all re-verified EXACT here
under @SN (lines in results.txt).  Of the 8 genuinely open ones, SIX are now EXACT: 41CB48, 41F298, 41C680, 427430, 427690, 42EF50 (recipes/hacks in their results
lines and in the sections below).  Still PARTIAL: func_0042C8AC (structure right, 29 register-operand diffs; wip/hard/func_0042C8AC.pin9.c) and func_00444024
(4 structural diffs, scheduling of one move + one lbu; wip/hard/func_00444024.c).  26 of 28 targets EXACT.
ORDER OF WORK THAT PAID OFF: (1) fix structure first (statement orientation, one shared increment, pointer locals), measured with tools/vm.py/sbsm; (2) brute-force
the order of the few statements around a 2-slot scheduling difference; (3) register pins (`register T v __asm__("$N")`) + ONE empty asm barrier for the rest;
(4) permuter last, from a base that is already within a handful of diffs (it solved 427690 in minutes from 2 structural, and did nothing for far-off bases).

TOOLING (new, tools/ of this workspace; run through WSL like the s507 tools)
- tools/sbsm.py <list> : aligned-diff metric (difflib on normalised mnemonics) for MANY list lines in one call; prints structural/operand counts plus
  firststruct/firstop and, with env WIN=lo,hi, the counts restricted to ROM insn window [lo,hi].
- tools/vm.py <variants.txt> <fn> <rom> [flags] : variants separated by //==== lines -> wip/vx_N.c -> ONE sbsm call -> sorted by structural*10+operand
  (WIN=lo,hi ranks by the window first; the best variant is copied to wip/vm_best.c).  Much better than chk's positional "N words differ" for big functions.
- tools/mvgen.py <file.c> <fn> <rom> <first_line> <last_line> [-m i,j] [-- flags] : statement-move search (each line = one statement; move statement i to every
  other position; one sbsm call; best -> wip/mv_best.c).  Brute-force statement order search in ~1 s per variant.
- tools/lst.py <list> <fn> [lo hi] : our insns | ROM insns by index, NO alignment (use when difflib is fooled by two similar chains).
- tools/sndump.sh <file.c> <dump letters> [cc1 flags] (WSL) -> /tmp/s706dump/s.c.<pass>; tools/rtlbrief.py <dump> prints the RTL stream compactly
  (uid, dest <- src) -> shows the stream order cse/loop/combine produced BEFORE sched1 (luid order) and the sched1/sched2 results.
- wip/pq.sh <secs> <jobs> fn:rom[:flag] ... : sequential decomp-permuter queue (own copy ~/permuter_s706/s706perm.py so nobody's pkill hits it); after each run the
  best output is copied to wip/c/pbest_<fn>.c and its sbsm metric printed.  Use the aligned metric to judge permuter output, not the permuter score.

SCHEDULER FACTS (gcc 2.8.1 sched.c, verified with the -dS dumps)
- haifa sched1/sched2 schedule BACKWARDS (log lines "ready list at T-n"): the insn with the highest priority (longest dependence path from the block START)
  is picked first and ends up LAST; ties go to the larger luid (later in the original stream).  An insn that becomes ready late and has low rank is picked last and
  therefore lands at the very TOP of its block.  This is why a 'move v1,a2' / a reload / an 'lbu' can sit at the block top in the ROM although the source uses it late:
  its LUID (stream position after cse/loop/reload) is early.  The luid of a constant load is the position of the FIRST use of that constant (cse creates the pseudo there).
- A load followed by a non-memory insn only: sched avoids placing a load directly before another memory insn (unit conflict); lbu/lwc1 end up before the next ALU insn.
- Because the order of insns inside one block is mostly stream order for equal priorities, moving statements in the SOURCE changes nothing for insns that have a
  dependent chain (loads feeding stores / FP chains); only an extra copy / different live range (INSN_REG_WEIGHT) changes their placement.

LOOP.C FACTS
- bivs are recorded in stream order and the iv_class list is built by PREPENDING, so the class discovered LAST is reduced FIRST; the giv-init insns are emitted
  before the loop in that order after the hoisted invariants (movables).  For func_00427430 the ROM emits the n-class (list+n*4) BEFORE the j-class (j*28):
  no natural source shape makes the n increments come after the j increment in the stream (checked: for/while/do, continue/explicit j++ arms, declaration order: all give
  j-class first).  Open.
- A loop pointer (`O3Pl4 *pp = &D[i]` declared INSIDE the loop body) turns an 'lui; addu $1,$1,off; lbu 120($1)' offset giv into the ROM's walking pointer
  (func_0041F298 last loop).

CODEGEN LESSONS
- func_0042C8AC:
  * `mode = f(); if (mode == 0) { switch (D_80154028[0]) { case 1: mode = 2; break; case 0: mode = 3; break; case 2: default: mode = 1; break; } }`
    reproduces the ROM's tree `beq g,1 ; slti g,2 ; beqz ; beql g,0` with `li` copies in the delay slots (the ROM tests ==1, <2, ==0): an if-chain gives another layout.
    The empty `case 2:` label (merged with default) is needed to get the median root.  D_80154028 must be an extern ARRAY (reloaded at every use).
  * sorting by key with two array elements: load the index, compare key by key IN SOURCE ORDER (q1,r1,ka then q2,r2,kb), not all four loads first.
  * the early 'lh f4' loads (hoisted above the f92 test) mean the source reads `a4 = e1->f4; b4 = e2->f4;` BEFORE the `if (e1->f92 != k || e2->f92 != k) continue;`
    and the tie-break compares those copies `(ka == kb && a4 < b4)`.
  * the 3-way mode test order/arm layout of the ROM (f8 arm first, f6 arm later, common f4 arm last, tests ==2, <3, !=3 not folded) only comes from a goto
    transcription: `if (mode == 2) goto m6; if (mode < 3) goto m4; if (mode != 3) goto m4; ka = f8..; goto cmp; m6: ..; goto cmp; m4: ..; cmp:`.
    `mode < 3 || mode != 3` is folded by fold_range_test into `mode != 3`; separate tests keep the hoisted `slti` invariant.
- func_00444024 / func_0041C680: the remaining diffs are 'hoisted to the block top' insns (see scheduler facts); putting the first const-1 store first moves the
  'move v1,a2' to the top (s607) but also moves the store.  Not solved.

NEW LESSONS (second half)
- SCHEDULING BARRIER + PRE-HOISTED TEMP (func_0041CB48 EXACT): an empty `__asm__("")` (or `__asm__ volatile("")`, `__asm__("" : : : "memory")`) right after a store splits the
  sched1/sched2 region.  Loads after it can no longer be hoisted above the stores before it (here lwc1 668(s3) stayed behind the `swc1 fC` store, as in the ROM), and a value
  that the ROM loads/converts EARLY (lui/lwc1 D_800E28D0 + cvt.s.w hoisted above the struct tail copy) must be computed into a temp BEFORE the barrier
  (`d28 = (f32)D_800E28D0; ...; a->fC = 10240.0f; __asm__(""); sc = base_f / d28;`).  A permuter-found `new_var = a` copy gave the same effect by a side route (it makes the store
  use another base register, so sched1 assumes a memory dependency); the barrier version leaves no extra insn.  A volatile store does NOT work, `do{}while(0)` does not either.
  The barrier also forces every loop-preheader insn that loop.c emits AFTER it below it, so it cannot be used inside a block whose preheader insns the ROM keeps at the block top (func_00444024).
- LOOP PREHEADER ORDER (func_00427430, structure now 0 words off): the constants 1 and -1 that loop.c normally hoists as MOVABLES (emitted first) can be made PLAIN code by
  naming them (`one = 1; neg = -1;` assigned right after `i = 0`, used inside the loop): then the order [i=0][li][li][sll;addu last][giv init of the j*28 class] follows source order
  (plain code first, loop.c giv inits last).  This is the pointer form `last = &sequence[count]` with the constants as locals; the remaining diff is the s0/s1 role swap
  (counter vs pointer) and `addu s1,v0,s6` operand order.
- Redundant `if (x > 0) { for (i = 0; i < x; ...) }` guards are NOT in the original when the ROM has one blez: the for's own entry test is enough (func_0042C8AC: removing the three
  `if (unkEC > 0)` and three `if (unkEC - 1 > 0)` wrappers cut 16 structural diffs).
- Two array elements fetched for a compare: load BOTH indices first (`q1 = d->unkF0[n]; q2 = d->unkF0[n+1]; e1 = &ent[q1]; e2 = &ent[q2];`), not e1 then e2 (func_0042C8AC).
- A `for (i = 0; i < 4; i++) if (D[i].f78 == 1) f(i);` turns into the ROM's walking-pointer loop only with `O3Pl4 *pp = &D_80146398[i];` declared INSIDE the body (func_0041F298).
- `t = x / 4; D->ta = t;` (not `ta = (x<0 ? x+3 : x); t = ta >> 2`): the ROM stores the quotient (func_0041F298).
- A permuter output can be semantically WRONG while scoring well: func_0042EF50 got 4 structural words by moving `G->unk14 = a * m` inside the `if` arm (the 900.0f path then never stores).
  Always diff the permuter's source against the base before adopting it.

LATE LESSONS (third batch)
- SCHED UNIT HAZARD (verified with -fsched-verbose=5 -dS/-dR, func_0042EF50): in the dump "blocking insn N for 1 cycles" means a LOAD (lbu/lw/lwc1) cannot be placed
  directly before a store in program order, so while the backward scheduler is still picking stores, every ready load is skipped and the loads end up at the very TOP of the
  block (lwc1 `m = 900.0f` before the six `G->unk.. = ` stores).  The ROM instead has that lwc1 right before the beq, i.e. its sched1 priority/luid was above the stores' (or the
  stores were not adjacent).  sched2 never repairs this: it re-numbers luids from the sched1 output, so what sched1 put at the top stays at the top.  A hand-written
  `register f32 m __asm__("$f0")` pin is not needed for the register (without it only $f0/$f2 operands differ), but the empty `__asm__("")` after `G->unk14 = a * m` IS needed
  (without it the lbu of the condition drifts: 8 structural diffs instead of 4).
- ALIGNED METRIC beats positional diffs: chk's "words differ" counts every word after one inserted insn; tools/sbsm.py (difflib on mnemonics) reports 4/0 for a function chk
  calls "13 words differ" (func_0042EF50) and 4/2 for one chk calls "113 words differ" (func_0041C680).
- REG COPY OF AN ARGUMENT (func_0041F298, open): the ROM keeps `key` (loaded once, stored to p->f10, passed to func_0041F248) in $v1 and copies it to $a0 in the jal delay slot
  (`lw $3,17108($1); ...; sw $3,16($2); jal; move $4,$3`).  Our cc1 always allocates the pseudo to $a0 directly (local-alloc copy suggestion), so the `move` disappears.  A
  `register s32 key __asm__("$3")` pin makes the move reappear but costs one extra insn elsewhere (295 vs 294) - not a solution.  Duplicating a store (`p->state = 0; ...;
  p->state = 0;`) found by the permuter shifts the ranks enough for the surrounding order but is not what the ROM has (ROM: a single `sw $0,20($2)`).
- INLINE FIND-LOOP LAYOUT (func_00427690, open): when the loop body ends with a "found" block that is followed by a later unconditional jump in the same loop, jump.c relocates
  the found block and the delay-slot filler produces `bnel ... ; j` where our source forms give `beql`.  Micro-tests wip/c/mf1..mf13.c reproduce both layouts but none of
  the natural source forms of the ROM's loop selects the bnel one.  Unifying the counter with the induction variable (`cnt` merged into `i`) removed 47 of 63 operand diffs.
- A permuter base should be kept CLEAN: wip/c/pbest_*.c files are preprocessed (3000+ lines); diff the permuter output against base.c with `diff -b -B <(sed 's/ *$//' ...)` and
  re-apply the 2-5 semantic changes to the clean source (done for 41F298 -> wip/hard/func_0041F298.c, 41C680 and 444024), then check the metric is unchanged.

SOLVED LATE (func_0042EF50 and func_0041F298 are EXACT @SN) - the recipes
- func_0042EF50: (1) A TERNARY INSIDE THE MULTIPLICATION `pr = a * ((C2D5 == 2 || C2D5 == 3) ? 15.0f : 900.0f);` is distributed by fold into the arms
  (`c ? a*15 : a*900`); each arm gets its own constant-load pseudo and sched1's INTERBLOCK (speculative) motion then hoists the 900.0f lwc1 into the branch block at the END
  (right before the beq), exactly like the ROM.  The `m = 900.0f; if (c) m = 15.0f;` and `m = c ? 15.0f : 900.0f;` forms keep the load in the branch block where it becomes the
  lowest-ranked load and is pushed to the block TOP (that was the 4 structural diffs of every earlier attempt).  (2) With the ternary inside the store RHS the store address is
  evaluated FIRST, so `G->unk14 = ...` creates a second pseudo `r95 = &D_801468A0` (copy of the first stores' base) living in another register
  (`lui $2;addiu;addu $8,$2,$0`).  Fix: hold the base in a LOCAL POINTER `O04GFlags *pg = G;` and use `pg->` everywhere including the lbu `((u8 *)pg)[-0x5CB]`.
  (3) The product lands in $f12 (the dying `a`, local-alloc tie) instead of the ROM's $f0: pin it `register f32 pr __asm__("$f0"); pr = a * (...); pg->unk14 = pr;`.
  (4) the single empty `__asm__("")` after the store is still needed (without it 2 structural diffs).
- func_0041F298: the ROM keeps `key` in $v1 and copies it in the jal delay slot.  `register s32 key __asm__("$3")` brings the `move $4,$3` back, but then sched1 places
  the move BEFORE the `p->f10 = key` store and reorg puts the store into the delay slot (`move; jal; sw $4`).  ONE empty `__asm__("")` between `p->f10 = key;` and
  `ch = func_0041F248(key);` keeps the store before the move (`sw $3; jal; move $4,$3`).  Pin + barrier = register choice + order fixes; each alone is not enough.
- GENERAL: when the ROM shows `move $a0,$vN` in a delay slot where our cc1 allocates the pseudo straight to $a0, use a register pin on the pseudo; then check the
  order of the surrounding stores and, if sched put the move first, add an empty asm between the store and the call.  Pins are visible to sched1 as hard-reg
  sets, so an anti-dependence can reorder things: always re-measure.
- A permuter output must be checked for TYPE changes: the 444024 run produced `unsigned char new_var` reused for the f32 `fv` (wrong code, scored 2/2).

LOOP / BRANCH LAYOUT LESSONS (func_00427690: 28 -> 2 structural diffs with these)
- loop.c RELOCATES a "found/break" block (`idx = i; goto done`) out of an inner loop to just after the next UNCONDITIONAL jump of the enclosing loop body
  (visible in the .loop dump as a new label after `jump N`; the cond jump becomes `beq ..., found`).  Then reorg emits `beq $2,$4,Lcall` with `move idx,i` in
  the delay slot instead of the ROM's `bnel $2,$4,cont ; i++ (annulled slot) ; j done ; move idx,i`.  The ROM layout appears when the enclosing loop body has NO
  unconditional jump after the inner loop: i.e. write the enclosing body with ONE shared increment at the end (`if (r == 1) { ... } i++; off += 12;`) instead of
  `if (r != 1) { i++; } else { ...; i++; ... }` (the duplicated i++ creates `jump end` barriers).  Doing this in both big loops removed 18 structural diffs at once.
- Orientation of an if/else decides `beql ... j` vs `bnel`: for `h->unk10 = 0xAA` (one insn) vs `{ h->unk10 = 0x46; c = 0x46; }` the ROM has
  `bnel $2,$22,Lend ; sb 170 (annulled slot) ; sb 70 ; li c,70 ; Lend:` = source `if (r == 1) { 0x46; c = 0x46; } else { 0xAA; }` (the one-insn else arm is copied into the
  slot of the inverted branch).  The `!= 1 ... else` spelling gives `beql ... ; j` + extra insn.
- A micro test that shows the find-loop shapes: with an inline `find()` whose result is NOT used again after the call, idx and the loop counter coalesce into one
  register and the found block is empty (`beq` layout, no bnel); if idx is used after the call (g6/h1 in wip/c/mf21.c, mf22.c) they stay in different registers and the
  `bnel/j` layout appears.  In func_00427690 idx is a single function-wide pseudo living in $17, so the register part was already right.
- A separately named offset variable per loop (`off` for the first big loop, `off2` for the last) was needed to get the ROM's different registers ($19 vs $20).
- PLAIN-CONSTANT LOCALS control the preheader order again: `one = 1; twenty = 0x14;` assigned between `i = 0;` and `off = 0;` and used inside the loop make the
  ROM order [i][c1][c20][off copy] (loop.c emits hoisted movables AFTER `i = 0` but before giv/biv copies only if they are already plain code).  The same trick on the last loop
  (70/170 consts) changed register pressure (an extra callee-saved register was no longer needed) and failed.

MORE RECIPES (func_0041C680 EXACT, func_00427690 EXACT, func_0042C8AC structure now 0)
- func_0041C680: a pointer that the ROM computes with `addiu $v0,$v0,8` in the blez DELAY SLOT and later copies (`addu $s4,$v0,$0`) is a value computed UNCONDITIONALLY before the
  `if (n > 0)` into its own local (`t2 = D_8011FE88->tab->ids;`) and only copied inside the branch (`e = t2;`).  When the computation sits inside the if-block, combine merges
  the add into the copy and the delay slot is filled with something else.  The relative statement order matters at the 2-slot level; a brute-force over the 120 orders of the 5
  prologue statements plus the 6 orders of the 3 statements in the if-block (tools/vm.py, ~1 min) found the exact order (`found = i = 0; n = ..->cnt; b = a + 0x190;
  new_var2 = 1; t2 = ..->ids;` and inside `lv = ..; one = new_var2; e = t2;`).  Do this brute force EARLY for any 2-slot scheduling difference in a short prologue.
- func_0042C8AC: for `kb = (r2 < 5) ? (D == 0 ? A : B) : -1` the ROM's `beq $2,$0 ; addiu $2,$0,-1` (plain slot, value computed into the same register as the condition) comes from
  `if (r2 >= 5) { kb = -1; } else if (D == 0) { kb = A; } else { kb = B; }` (the ka copy of this shape already was that); the `if (r2 < 5) { ... } else kb = -1` spelling puts the -1 block after the join
  and gives `beql ... ; j ; slt` duplicates.  Together with pins on the block-local ka/kb/q1/q2 (`register s32 bkb __asm__("$2")` etc.) this took the function from 7 structural to 0 (only registers left).
  The ROM's `lh $7,212($4)` / `lbu $2,354($4)` (field displacement = 0xD0 + field, base = the bare symbol in a register) needs the cfg pointer in a LOCAL VARIABLE (`cfg3 = D_801462C8;` then `cfg3->ent[q].f4`
  without an `e1 = &cfg->ent[q]` temp): D_801462C8->ent[q] directly gives `lui/addiu (sym+208)` as base and displacement = field only.
- `wip/pq.sh`-style permuter runs are most effective AFTER the structure is right: func_00427690 went from 2 structural to EXACT in a few minutes (the permuter found `twenty = off2;`
  as a dead-variable reuse that moves one copy by two slots), after 28 -> 2 had been done by hand.
- REGISTER-CASCADE DEBUGGING (func_0042C8AC; method that took 136 -> 6 operand diffs): (1) fix all structural diffs first; (2) read the aligned diff top to bottom and fix the FIRST register
  mismatch only, then re-run (each fix usually removes dozens of later diffs because global-alloc hands out registers in priority order); (3) the usual fixes were: give each loop FAMILY its own
  variable name (the ROM reuses one register for the variables the original source reused: here i for 3 loops, j/n for 2 groups, a different pair in the third group - allocating one function-wide
  `i` for everything gave $12 where the ROM has $10 in the first loops); (4) pin non-induction variables with `register s32 v __asm__("$N")` (induction variables must NOT be pinned: loop.c
  does not strength-reduce hard-register bivs and the loop code changes completely); (5) address temporaries that the ROM keeps in $v0 while our cc1 ties them to the dying index register need
  an explicit pinned temp (`register s32 *ap __asm__("$2"); ap = (s32 *)((u8 *)GA + x * 4); ka = ap[16];`); (6) a compare result that ties to a pinned dying operand register (`slt $7,$7,$5`) needs
  an explicit pinned result (`(ka == kb && (cc = a4 < b4))` with `register s32 cc __asm__("$2")`).  tools/vm.py runs ~100 variants in 2-3 minutes, so a greedy pin search over (variable x register) is cheap (wip/greedy.py).
- Hoisted loop movables are emitted in ENCOUNTER order of the loop body scan (cfg pointer assigned in the body before the inner loop => emitted before the D_800E53C0 pointer that is hoisted from the
  inner loop), so a pointer local assigned at the top of an outer loop lands BEFORE the other invariant loads in the preheader; the ROM sometimes has it after (func_0042C8AC group 2, open).
--- s707 ---
FINDINGS s707 (targets: func_00444320, func_0044632C; both already solved by earlier workers, re-verified here with chk @SN)
- func_00444320 is not a function: it is the case-3 loop body of func_00444260 (jump-table label split into a fake symbol); chk on the fake name says "not found in object", but func_00444260 (61 insns, 0x1B2260..0x1B2354) is EXACT @SN and covers it.
- func_0044632C is EXACT @SN from the s607 source (void return, inlined func_004467D8 as do{c=*a++;d=*b++;n--;}while(c==d&&c!=0&&n==0); return c==d;). All earlier PARTIALs (o06, q04, s411, s508) returned 8-cnt; the ROM function returns void, which fixes the li/subu tail scheduling. The old-flags version needed an `asm("$6")` pin for the inlined helper; the SN version needs none.
- Machine load was ~37 (WSL load average); a single fchk call took >2 minutes.

=== ROUND 8 FINDINGS (workspace s806) ===
FINDINGS s806 (round 8, real toolchain @SN; targets func_004351A0 ... func_00444024) -- new lessons only

RESULT: all 10 targets EXACT under @SN (func_0043C4F4 = span file with 21 members), ALL WITHOUT HACKS: no asm, no register pins, no volatile.  The two functions that
earlier drafts matched only with hacks (func_0043F69C: f32 register pin + asm barrier; func_00444024: 4/0 plateau, then volatile + pin + early inits) are now natural source.

HEADLINE LESSONS (check these first on every near-miss)
A. DECLARE READ-ONLY POOL SYMBOLS `const`.  `extern const f32 D_800E2798;` makes the load RTX_UNCHANGING_P: sched/alias then add NO dependence between it and any store.
   `extern f32 D_800E2798;` (non-const) is "may alias" with every store through a pointer parameter: every store written BEFORE the read is pinned before it and every store
   after it behind it.  func_00444024 was a 4-structural plateau for three workers and needed volatile + a register pin + early initialised declarations (all dead ends, see 10)
   until the symbol was made const: then plain source in ROM order matches (statement order below).  Applies to any pool constant (float or integer table) that the
   original code read as a literal/const object; try it before volatile, pins, asm or the permuter.
B. `do { } while (0)` (or wrapping one statement in it) is a natural scheduling barrier: the LOOP_BEG/LOOP_END notes make the first insn after them a barrier that depends on
   everything before it, so nothing moves across (same effect as `__asm__ volatile("")`, which earlier workers used 10+ times; probably the original macro form).
   func_0043F69C: `do { o->f4 = (o->f4 * D_800E28D0[0]) / 284; } while (0);` stops sched2 from hoisting `lw v1,4(s2)` above the `trunc.w.s/swc1` pair (an EMPTY
   `do { } while (0);` between the statements works too; a bare `{ }` block does not).
C. A REGISTER PIN ON A TEMPORARY IS USUALLY THE WRONG FIX (func_0043F69C `register f32 fq asm("$f0")`).  Cause: local-alloc ranks quantities by refs / life length; sched1 puts
   the `(set r (float:SF (mem)))` insn (lwc1+cvt.s.w) after a plain load (larger potential hazard => picked first backward => placed last), so in `E->f8 * (f32)D_800E28D4`
   the f8 pseudo lives 3 insns, the height 2, the height wins $f0 and everything is swapped.  The ROM allocation needs sched1 order [height, f8, mul], i.e. the height's
   def UNBOOSTED: make it a NAMED temp that is assigned in several branch arms (multi-set pseudo): `hh = (f32)D_800E28D4;` in the flags arm (`v = hh;`), in the mode==2 arm
   (`v = (E->f8 + 0.5f) * hh;`) and in the else arm (`v = E->f8 * hh;`) -> EXACT, no pin (mode==2 + else arms alone also work; else alone / flags + else do not).  sched2 then swaps
   the two loads back by itself (forcing the order with a barrier instead fixes the registers but sched2 cannot swap: 2 structural off).  Diagnostics: s.c.lreg
   "Register N used x times across y insns" and the sched1 order of the two loads (wip/sh/cmp3.sh).
D. cse deletes a store that is IMMEDIATELY followed (adjacent insns) by a store to the same address.  The ROM keeps `unk4 = 0; ... unk4 = 0x4000000; ... unk4 = -1` (three stores)
   because another statement sits between the last two in the source: `a->unk10 = 0;` (a free store that sched2 later dumps elsewhere).  When a ROM shows apparently dead
   double stores, look for the statement between them, not for volatile.

func_00444024 STATEMENT ORDER THAT MATCHES (all non-volatile, const global):
   `a->unk17 = 1;` FIRST (see E), then unk18, unk19, unk1A, unk14 (0x80 etc.), unk8, unk0 = 0, unk4 = 0, unk0 = 0x4000000, unk4 = 0x4000000, unk10 = 0 (the separator, D),
   unk4 = -1, unk1B = 1, unk1C, unk1F, unk580 = 0, unk24..27 = 10, unk28 = 1, unk29, unk2A, unk2B = 1, `a->unk16 = a->unk14;` (narrows to lbu 21), `a->unk20 = D_800E2798;`
   (read at its use), natural `for (i = 0; i < 10; i++) a->unk2C[i] = 1;`, then the two inlined func_00444204 calls.
E. WHY `move v1,a2` sits at the very top of the ROM function (idx 7) although the four `sb v1,..` stores are 27..40 insns later: the constant `1` of the four byte stores (pseudo A)
   is created by cse at the FIRST use of that constant, so the first source statement `a->unk17 = 1;` gives A the lowest luid; sched1 releases A last (its first user is the first
   chain store), boosts it and puts its `li` in front of the first chain `li 128`; the loop-1 invariant `li a2,1` (hoisted by loop.c, unboosted) and the biv/giv inits float
   to the top group in front of it; reload_cse turns A's `li` into `move v1,a2`.  Then sched2 sinks the FREE stores (stores of $0 / of v1 are not tied to the v0 `li` chain) into the
   first gap behind `li v0,10` (ROM idx 33-39: `sb v1,23; sw zero,16; sb v1,27; sb zero,28; sb zero,31; sb zero,1408`).  With a non-const global this first store is pinned
   BEFORE the `lwc1` (store -> load of possibly aliasing memory), which is exactly the s706 draft's 4/0 plateau (`sb v1,27` at idx 20 instead of 36; lbu after the lwc1).

SCHEDULER MODEL (verified with -fsched-verbose=5 -dS / -dR on these functions; gcc 2.8.1 sched.c streamed from the gcc-2.8.1 tag, never saved)
- birthing boost: adjust_priority() runs only when an insn is RELEASED by dependence (last successor scheduled) and only before reload; if its dest pseudo is in bb_live_regs and
  REG_N_SETS == 1 its priority becomes max_priority = LAUNCH_PRIORITY 0x7f000001 (dump: "uid (7f000001)").  Insns in the INITIAL ready list (no successor in the block: hoisted loop
  invariants, biv/giv inits, an `li` whose only use is in the next block) are never boosted; with rank (priority, class, luid) they are picked only when no store is ready, i.e. at
  the END of the backward pass = the TOP of the block, in descending luid pick order (ascending in program order: invariant `li`, biv init, giv init).
- schedule_select prefers (same priority) the insn with the larger potential hazard = STORES over ALU insns, and a `(set r (float:SF (mem)))` insn over a plain load; a LOAD is
  "blocked for 1 cycle" after every store pick and launched only right after an ALU pick ("launching 120 before 84"): a boosted load lands directly before the first ALU insn
  (usually a boosted `li`) that follows its release, NOT next to its use; an unboosted or pinned load sinks to the top of the block.
- a store that consumes a load result has priority 2 (load latency 2) and is picked first = placed LAST in the block (`sb a0,22`, `swc1 f0,32` at the end of func_00444024).
- sched2 has no boost, but register REUSE after allocation creates the dependences: constants sharing $v0 form a rigid li/store chain (li K2 must follow the store of K1: WAR),
  while stores of $0 / of another register are FREE: every ready free store is taken before the next `li` (store > ALU), so all free stores below the chain element are dumped at the
  first gap, consecutively, in luid order.
- reload_cse_regs turns a later `li` of a constant that another hard reg holds into `move` (not for the `li` reload itself emits for an asm operand); `li a2 ... move v1,a2` therefore
  proves that the loop-invariant `li` has the LOWER luid in the sched2 stream.
- cse merges a later same-EBB `1` into an earlier `1` (also across modes via lowpart), so two registers holding 1 in one block need different EBBs/hard regs.

TOOLING (all in tools/ or wip/sh/, run through WSL; there is NO Windows python)
- tools/w.sh "<cmd>"  runs <cmd> in WSL at the workspace root.  Traps: (a) `$var` / `\$var` inside the quoted command are expanded by the outer shells and arrive EMPTY -> put
  loops/variables into a script file (wip/sh/*.sh) and call `tools/w.sh "bash wip/sh/x.sh args"`; (b) the Bash tool collapses backslashes (`\n` becomes `n`) -> wip/sh/mkvar.py uses the
  token @NL@ for a newline; (c) a foreground call that runs > 120 s is moved to the background (use run_in_background, then Read the output file).
- tools/vb.py <fn> <rom hex> [--flags "@SN"] [--jobs N] [--top N] (--dir D | --file variants.txt | files...)  : PARALLEL variant ranking with the aligned metric (structural =
  inserted/deleted/changed-mnemonic insns, operand = same mnemonic but other register/immediate; relocations masked).  It skips lu_match's 8 MB ROM index build, so a variant costs
  ~2-3 s on an idle box (use --jobs 3-6 when other agents load the machine: load average was 40+ on 16 cores).  Best variant -> wip/vb_best.c.
- wip/sh/mkvar.py base out 'old=>new'... : text-replace variant generator (a hypothesis test = 5 variants + vb.py in one call, ~15 s).  wip/sh/exp5.py : every single-statement
  MOVE (or swap) inside a statement block -> directory of variants (vb.py --dir; 676 variants ~25 min under load).  wip/sh/exp13.py : `do { } while (0)` before / around every statement.
- wip/sh/dumpall.sh <file.c> (all RTL stages), show.sh / lines.sh / grepst.sh / around.sh / cmp3.sh / cmp4.sh (condensed one-line RTL of any stage), stages.sh, sverb.sh <file.c>
  -dS|-dR sched|sched2 (scheduler log: "ready list at T-n: uid (priority)", "blocking insn", "launching n before m", "insn n has a greater potential hazard", "now ..." = pick order,
  insn[uid]: priority =, ref_count =).  Stage files: s.c.rtl/jump/cse/loop/cse2/combine/sched/lreg/greg/sched2/jump2.  tools/rtlc.py condenses a dump to one line per insn.
  wip/sh/diffonly.sh = side-by-side restricted to the differing regions.
- tools/constsweep.py <file.c> <fn> <rom hex> [--jobs N] : lesson A as a tool: one variant per non-function `extern` made `const` (+ all-const), ranked with vb.py next to the
  base.  On the non-const func_00444024 source: base 12 structural / 14 operand, `extern const f32 D_800E2798;` variant EXACT.  Run it on every near-miss that reads extern data.
- tools/syms.py <file.c> [tokens] : list the function symbols (name, offset, size) a file produces (proves that a `static inline` helper emits no out-of-line copy).
- tools/rdis.sh <rom hex> <n> : disassemble ROM words.  wip/sh/tgrep.py / tshow.py : grep / print a subagent transcript jsonl (recover text after a context reset).

OTHER LESSONS
5. `static inline` helper copies inside a group file must be `static`: a plain `inline` helper that the lead integrates separately as its own function is emitted a second time
   out-of-line (check with tools/syms.py).  Verified for func_0043F290/0043F404/00442E80 (typed getter/setter helpers).
6. Verification recipe used for every result: `bash tools/fk.sh list` (chk) AND for functions with a rodata pool `python3 tools/rocheck.py file fn rom @SN` (pool base must be one
   value; differing words may only be jump-table words, which are relocations in our object).
7. An EMPTY asm("+r") on a pseudo that holds a constant is optimised away by combine (the constant is folded into the asm input "0[ 1 ]"); only a global register variable
   (`register T x __asm__("$3")`) keeps the `li` as a separate, unboosted hard-reg set.  Redundant plain re-assignments of such a variable are deleted by cse.
8. When a context reset hides earlier reasoning, the subagent transcript can be searched with wip/sh/tgrep.py (pattern, context, max hits); the sched.c source can be streamed
   (never saved) with `curl -s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/gcc-mirror/gcc/releases/gcc-2.8.1/gcc/sched.c | sed -n 'a,bp'` (birthing_insn_p 2499, adjust_priority 2545,
   rank_for_schedule 2415, schedule_select 2647, schedule_block 3173, bb_live_regs updates 3444-3680 / 3760-3900).
9. A helper that exists both inlined and standalone (func_00444204, 23 insns at ROM 0x1B2204): look for ONE body order that matches in both contexts.  The order
   unk78, unk79, unk7A, unk7C, unk7B, unk7D, unk7E, unk7F, unk80, unk82, unk83, unk81, unk94, unk95 (then the call of func_00444260) is EXACT inlined in func_00444024 (the
   inline stores keep source order there, there is no reordering room) AND as the standalone function, while o06's delivered standalone order (79, 7A, 78, ...) matches
   only standalone (EXACT inlined needs 5 operand fixes).  The group file defines it `static inline`; the lead can use the same body for the standalone func_00444204.
10. DEAD ENDS FOR func_00444024 (kept in wip/hard/func_00444024.va2_volatile_pin.c, all exact but unnecessary): `volatile O06B *` param (rigid chain, explicit byte read
   `((volatile u8 *)a)[0x15]` because a volatile s16 read is not narrowed to lbu), `register u8 one __asm__("$3") = 1;` + empty asm("+r") (unboosted A), initialised declarations
   `u8 two = 1; s32 i = 9; u8 *p = ...;` + a pointer-walking for loop (loop invariants earlier in the stream than A).  They compensated for the pinned load; with `const` the
   plain source matches.  Lesson: if a pool symbol is read in the function, test `const` BEFORE trying volatile/pins; and when the rigid ROM order looks "volatile", remember
   that sched2 itself makes $v0-chain stores rigid and dumps free stores into gaps.

  wip/sh/diffonly.sh = side-by-side restricted to the differing regions.
- tools/constsweep.py <file.c> <fn> <rom hex> [--jobs N] : lesson A as a tool: one variant per non-function `extern` made `const` (+ all-const), ranked with vb.py next to the
  base.  On the non-const func_00444024 source: base 12 structural / 14 operand, `extern const f32 D_800E2798;` variant EXACT.  Run it on every near-miss that reads extern data.
- tools/syms.py <file.c> [tokens] : list the function symbols (name, offset, size) a file produces (proves that a `static inline` helper emits no out-of-line copy).
- tools/rdis.sh <rom hex> <n> : disassemble ROM words.  wip/sh/tgrep.py / tshow.py : grep / print a subagent transcript jsonl (recover text after a context reset).

OTHER LESSONS
5. `static inline` helper copies inside a group file must be `static`: a plain `inline` helper that the lead integrates separately as its own function is emitted a second time
   out-of-line (check with tools/syms.py).  Verified for func_0043F290/0043F404/00442E80 (typed getter/setter helpers).
6. Verification recipe used for every result: `bash tools/fk.sh list` (chk) AND for functions with a rodata pool `python3 tools/rocheck.py file fn rom @SN` (pool base must be one
   value; differing words may only be jump-table words, which are relocations in our object).
7. An EMPTY asm("+r") on a pseudo that holds a constant is optimised away by combine (the constant is folded into the asm input "0[ 1 ]"); only a global register variable
   (`register T x __asm__("$3")`) keeps the `li` as a separate, unboosted hard-reg set.  Redundant plain re-assignments of such a variable are deleted by cse.
8. When a context reset hides earlier reasoning, the subagent transcript can be searched with wip/sh/tgrep.py (pattern, context, max hits); the sched.c source can be streamed
   (never saved) with `curl -s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/gcc-mirror/gcc/releases/gcc-2.8.1/gcc/sched.c | sed -n 'a,bp'` (birthing_insn_p 2499, adjust_priority 2545,
   rank_for_schedule 2415, schedule_select 2647, schedule_block 3173, bb_live_regs updates 3444-3680 / 3760-3900).
9. A helper that exists both inlined and standalone (func_00444204, 23 insns at ROM 0x1B2204): look for ONE body order that matches in both contexts.  The order
   unk78, unk79, unk7A, unk7C, unk7B, unk7D, unk7E, unk7F, unk80, unk82, unk83, unk81, unk94, unk95 (then the call of func_00444260) is EXACT inlined in func_00444024 (the
   inline stores keep source order there, there is no reordering room) AND as the standalone function, while o06's delivered standalone order (79, 7A, 78, ...) matches
   only standalone (EXACT inlined needs 5 operand fixes).  The group file defines it `static inline`; the lead can use the same body for the standalone func_00444204.
10. DEAD ENDS FOR func_00444024 (kept in wip/hard/func_00444024.va2_volatile_pin.c, all exact but unnecessary): `volatile O06B *` param (rigid chain, explicit byte read
   `((volatile u8 *)a)[0x15]` because a volatile s16 read is not narrowed to lbu), `register u8 one __asm__("$3") = 1;` + empty asm("+r") (unboosted A), initialised declarations
   `u8 two = 1; s32 i = 9; u8 *p = ...;` + a pointer-walking for loop (loop invariants earlier in the stream than A).  They compensated for the pinned load; with `const` the
   plain source matches.  Lesson: if a pool symbol is read in the function, test `const` BEFORE trying volatile/pins; and when the rigid ROM order looks "volatile", remember
   that sched2 itself makes $v0-chain stores rigid and dumps free stores into gaps.

=== ROUND 9 FINDINGS: RODATA (lead, 2026-10-10) ===
Why 23 "EXACT" results (about 7000 insns) could not be integrated, and how they were fixed.  chk compared CODE only; a pool slot is just an offset in an instruction word, so
float/double VALUES, pool ORDER, the alignment pad before a double and extra out-of-line copies were never verified.  Tools: tools/chk.py (now also checks the pool), tools/rodoc.py
(rodata doctor: whole-piece diff against the ROM, --place slides the pool over the ROM), tools/rdprep.py / rdone.py / rdall.py / rdintegrate.py (repair loop over wip/rd/).
1. chk now prints `CODE-EXACT, POOL-ORDER(slot bases ...)` (the ROM code reads its pool entries at distances that differ from ours) or `CODE-EXACT, POOL-VALUE(+off ours X rom Y)`.  Neither is
   EXACT.  ro=0xROM:size printed by chk is the pool piece (start = first slot the ROM code reads, size = our pool incl. alignment pads).
2. A double is 8-aligned in memory.  The ORIGINAL rodata of a translation unit started 8-aligned, so the pad before a `.double` depends on the ABSOLUTE address of the piece, not on its
   offset inside our one-function object.  tools/sn_abs.py now computes every `.align` pad for the piece address (ro_va); chk retries with each base the references suggest (pool at 4
   mod 8 -> the pad moves).  Fixed that way: func_00402BA0 (ro 0xE1854:76), func_0026195C, func_0023D370, func_00441764 (0xE30E4:32), func_002ABED4.
3. An unreferenced pool word in front of the first referenced one (func_002ABED4: a dead 1.0 at 0xCBFB8) is NOT part of the piece; the piece starts at the first slot the ROM reads
   (0xCBFBC); the word stays in the ROM data blob.
4. Helpers defined in the file for inlining must be `static inline` (plain `inline` emits an extra out-of-line function AND its constants into the object: the real build sees a bigger
   object / extra pool words).  Found in func_00215868, 002518DC, 00282A18, 002849C4, 002856B0, 002A8DD4, 002AB19C (5 helpers).  Check with `python3 tools/rodoc.py` (prints the function list).
5. Constants the original keeps in the function's own pool must be LITERALS: workers had written `extern f32 D_800C9B88` for pool floats (0.5, 2.3748726e-06f, 0.021052632f ...).  The
   code then still matches but the object pool lacks the entry (func_00276D20: dbl, FLT, pad, dbl; func_0027DD1C: 0.021052632f first).  Use the ROM bytes to find the value:
   tools/rodoc.py prints the float of every differing word.
6. Wrong literal values hidden behind identical code: func_0021CF04 (0.75 vs 0.5, last two uses), func_002743F0 (0.00017453294f is 2 ulps from 0.0001745329f), func_002A98E0 (the SWITCH
   case ORDER decides the jump table AND the pool order: cases 0,1,3,4/5,2/6,default).  The jump table of a switch is rodata too: the order of the case bodies in the source matters.
7. Pools of several functions that the ROM keeps contiguous but the compiler gets from one TU may contain foreign data (func_0043C4F4 span: 0xB0 bytes of resident strings between the
   first function's jump table and the second one's).  Split the span at such gaps: object 1 = func_0043C500 (ro 0xE2C80:132), object 2 = func_0043C730..0043D4E0 (903 insns, ro 0xE2DB8:348).
8. Upstream (melalawi) does not derive rodata from code at all: constants and strings are separate C data objects (rw_constants_*, rw_pool_*, ragewars_resident_strings_*) placed at
   fixed ROM addresses, with packed structs.  That avoids every layout problem above but needs one data file per piece; we keep compiler-generated pools where they reproduce.

=== ROUND 9b FINDINGS (s901 s903 s904 s905 s906: allocator rules, sched1, preferences, pins) (workspace s901) ===
ROUND 9 / s901 - lessons from func_00269A80 (1134 insns, switch + F3DEX display-list pushes; PARTIAL, 5 words off) and func_00200610 (handed to the lead)
(only lessons that are NOT already in FINDINGS_all.txt; all verified with the real @SN toolchain)

LAYOUT / SCHEDULING
1. Operand load drifting behind a div (sched1 gap fill).  Block = `call rand ; lw n0 ; lw n1 ; div(v0,n0) ; beqz n1`.  Writing the copy BEFORE the condition
   (`v = u; if (n1 == 0 || v >= n1)`) puts `v = u` (a single-set-less pseudo, priority 38 = 35-cycle div latency + 3) at the end of the call block; sched1 (backward)
   launches that copy first, the div then cannot issue for 35 cycles and the ready `lw n1` fills the gap, i.e. ends up BEHIND the div.  Writing the copy inside the
   second operand (`if (n1 == 0 || (v = u) >= n1)`) moves it into the fall-through block, the call block then is lw n0 / lw n1 / div / beqz exactly like the ROM
   (rand arm of func_00269A80, 2 structural words -> 0).  Read the log with tools/schedlog.sh <dump.sched> <uid> (ready lists per block).
2. A `div`/`mod` PARALLEL whose quotient is unused is single_set() = the mod set: the dying divisor shares the quotient's register (`div zero,s0,v0` with n0 in v0),
   and expand_preferences() merges the preferences of a pseudo dying in that insn with the destination of the mod: a short-lived temp that dies in `v = idx % t`
   inherits the preferred register of v (here a3 = 4th call argument).  So `t = info.n1; v = idx % t` lands in a3 in every arm and the identical arm tails are
   cross-jumped.  A holder variable that lives on (c1, t2) gets a different register and keeps the tails apart (the ROM has the tails split: n1 in a0/a3/a1).
3. `x = 0; if (c) x = 2;` becomes srl/andi (jump.c store-flag) unless the 0 is not a constant: `c0 = zero;` with a function-scope `s32 zero = 0;` keeps the
   ROM's `beqz ; addu t0,zero,zero (slot) ; addiu t0,zero,2`.  A 2-insn arm or a label between also blocks it.
4. Copy + two ORs (`c1 = c0; if (f) { c0 |= 1; c1 |= 1; }`): with the ROM's order (base first) cse merges the second OR into a copy (`ori v1,v1,1 ; addu t0,v1,zero`)
   because the copy c1 becomes the canonical register of the quantity (its last use, the 8th stack arg, is after c0's and beyond the cse block).  A dead
   `c0 = zero;` as the very last statement of the function raises last_uid(c0) and the ROM's `ori t0 ; ori v1` comes out (known trick, q03 - here it also flips the
   ORDER of the two ORs).  The dead statement is deleted before flow, nothing else changes.
5. Display-list pushes (F3DEX): `P4Gfx *gN = D_80110634++; gN->words.w0 = ..; gN->words.w1 = ..;` with a DISTINCT pointer variable per push, grey/3 value computed
   BEFORE the push, float globals read through one aggregate (struct of f32 c0..c4) so that the loads keep their order behind the stores (MEM_IN_STRUCT rule).

TOOLS (new, in tools/)
- wvb.py / wip/exp.py: spec file (BASE, VARS = {name: [(old, new), ...]}) -> variants -> aligned-diff metric (structural / operand / first divergence / window).
  This turned out far better than the permuter for 5-30 word problems: 40 variants in ~1 minute.
- schedlog.sh (sched1 ready-list log of the block containing uid N), rtlregion.sh / rtlgrep.sh (condensed RTL around a regex for any dump letter), dual.py + cols.py
  (side by side ours/ROM with branch targets as insn numbers), fsweep_sn.sh (single extra cc1 flags under @SN), killperm.sh (stop my permuter without matching itself).

NEGATIVE RESULTS (do not repeat)
- Remaining diff of func_00269A80: ROM keeps the default arm's n1 in a1, ours (any holder tried) in v1/a3/t1.  Not fixed by: ~60 holder variables (every existing
  local, s32 and u32; u32 turns `idx % n1` into divu), fresh locals at 18 declaration positions, `v = 0` first (sched1 hoists it to the arm start, reorg then moves it
  into the dispatch jump's delay slot), sharing a1v/a3v (the tail masks, which live in a1/a3 in the ROM) with the arms' n1 temps (they swap), register pins
  (`register s32 n1 asm("$5")` makes every arm choose a1 and breaks the rand arm: the explicit hard reg adds a1 to the preferences of the other arms' temps),
  declaration order of all locals (no effect), the 24 single cc1 flags of fsweep_sn.sh, 17000 permuter iterations with the default arm randomized.
- Empty `__asm__("" : : "r"(n1h))` after the switch (to make the default arm's n1 live through the w/h code so that v1/a0 are excluded and a1 is the first fit):
  the holder is then undefined on the other arms, i.e. live-in at function entry and across both calls -> it takes a callee-saved register (s3) and shifts the
  prologue.  Needs a def on every path without emitting an insn - not found.
  Analysis: with standard global-alloc rules n1 should be v1 (v0 = n0) or a3 (merge with v); a1 needs either hard-reg conflicts with v1 and a0 or an a1 copy
  preference that I could not find in the ROM code of insns 149-178.
