=== ROUND 9b FINDINGS (s901 s903 s904 s905 s906: allocator rules, sched1, preferences, pins) (workspace s903) ===
FINDINGS s903 (round 9, @SN toolchain; targets func_0023A4D0 [708 insns] and func_0040BE38 [185 insns]) -- new lessons only
RESULT: both EXACT under @SN (func_0040BE38 needs -fno-builtin and three register pins, see 7).

0. WORKFLOW THAT WORKED (both drafts were old-flag near-misses of earlier workers; re-run them under @SN FIRST: func_0040BE38_sa6 went from "198 insns, 198 words
   off" to 2 words off just by adding `@SN -fno-builtin`; the ROM calls memcpy, @SN without -fno-builtin inlines the 0x50 byte copy).
   Use the ALIGNED metric (structural / operand diff counts) for every step; the positional "N words differ" of chk is meaningless once an insn is shifted.
   func_0023A4D0: n16 draft (16 structural, 7 operand) -> EXACT in ~12 steps, each step below was a 1-line source change.

1. FLOAT POOL = LITERALS.  func_0023A4D0 references each of its 9 pool constants exactly once (768.0 5120.0 10240.0 0.25 0.75 1.0 0.5 16384.0 and the implicit 2^31 of the
   (u32) casts); ROM shows `lui at,%hi(D_800C86A0+4)` style addressing = entries of the function's OWN pool.  Writing them as plain float literals (not `extern f32 D_800C86xx`)
   gives the same code AND chk reports `ro=0xC92A0:36` (whole pool, order and values verified by the new checker + rocheck.py).  The extern version was also code-EXACT but
   its ro= piece was only the 4-byte 2^31 entry.  The pool order is the order of first use in the SOURCE (A0,A4,A8,AC,B0,B4,B8,BC,C0), so keep the expression order.
   A repeated 0.25f (two source uses) is CSE'd into one load/one pool entry, as in the ROM.

2. `asm("" : : "r"(x))` (no output operand) is implicitly VOLATILE: a full scheduling barrier.  The earlier draft used two of them to "fix" the s0/s2/s3 assignment of the three
   switch indices a0/a1/a2, which cost 16 structural diffs elsewhere (hoisted constants and `i = 0` could no longer float to the block top).  What the asm really did was add
   references to a0: global-alloc priority is floor_log2(weighted refs)*refs/live_length, REG_N_REFS is weighted by loop depth (x2 inside the q4 do-while) and a0 had 7 refs vs
   9 for a1/a2 (14 vs 18 weighted: floor_log2 = 3 vs 4).  ONE `__asm__("" : "+r"(a0));` after the switch (non-volatile, an output operand, 2 refs, not a barrier) lifts a0 to 9 refs
   (tie with a1, lower pseudo number wins) and gives the ROM's s0/s2/s3 = a0/a1/a2 with the schedule untouched (30 operand diffs -> 0).  Generic recipe for a pure
   callee-saved permutation: count the refs per pseudo (tools/pr903.py), find the pseudo whose floor_log2 bucket is one too low, add refs with an empty asm("+r").

3. `x = cond ? a : b;` stored to MEMORY (`alp[i] = (i == 0) ? al : 0.0f;`) expands the target address BEFORE the compare and stores each arm directly into it, so cse1 does not
   apply the path knowledge `i == 0` to the address: ROM `swc1 f4,0(t0)` (strength-reduced pointer).  The if/else with two statements `alp[i] = a; ` / `alp[i] = 0.0f;` folds the
   then-arm address to `192(sp)` (constant index).  Use the ternary when the ROM stores through the walking pointer in both arms.
   Also reuse the variable (`al *= unk208 * unk220;` instead of a new a0v) -> `mul.s f4,f4,f0` in place, store from the same FP register.

4. Natural statement order wins over "scheduled" order: write the vector copy as x, y, z (`pts[i].x = v->pos[i].x; pts[i].y = ey; pts[i].z = ...`): the ROM's load of x before the
   store of y comes from the independent store filling the load delay slot; writing y first keeps the load behind the (may-alias) store.

5. LOOP PREHEADER ORDER (unboosted initial-ready insns are emitted in luid order, higher-latency FP loads last): `i = 1;` BEFORE `p0 = &pts[0];` and an explicit FP variable
   `zero = 0.0f;` BEFORE `lim = 1.0f;` (both plain statements before the loop, loop uses `for (; i < 5; i++)` and compares `dd >= zero`) gives the ROM order
   [li i][p0][giv init][mtc1 zero][lwc1 lim]; the implicit hoisted 0.0 constant of loop.c is emitted after the explicit loads.

6. Two loads whose order is wrong although registers are right (lwc1 536(s7) vs 524(s7)): move BOTH loads textually to just after `cls = e->cls;` (tt0 then tt1) and drop them
   from their old places; moving one of them only changes the FP register numbers.  (luid order of boosted loads at the block top = order of the assignments.)

7. func_0040BE38 (os.vi mode editor).  The last 2 words are local-alloc, not scheduling: `lh a1,16(sp)` (ROM) vs `lh v1,16(sp)` (every natural source).  local-alloc facts
   (modelled in tools/lamodel.py, which reproduces the real choices of this block): output ties to the FIRST dying input (a - b ties sub to a, so the three-insn
   expression a/sub/sra chain is ONE quantity in v0); quantities are allocated by floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/(death-birth), ties by birth order, first-fit over regs 2..;
   b gets v1 unless v1 is busy over [lh b, subu].  The ROM has `subu v1,a0,a3` / `addu v1,v1,v0` (hm - hw + xb/2) live across that point, i.e. in the ROM's sched1 output the
   c0 add and the `hs = c0 << 16` shift sit AFTER the halfword loads (sched2 later hoists them to the top; the model confirms b -> a1 when insn 156 is moved after the lh).
   No natural source variant (1600 single statement moves, 96 empty-asm insertions, 56K permuter iterations, many expression forms) reproduces that sched1 order, because the
   shift's pseudo (`hs`, multi-set) is unboosted and floats to the block top.  EXACT form:  three register pins on the temporaries of the first block only
        register s32 bb __asm__("$5"); register s32 aa __asm__("$2"); register u32 cc __asm__("$8");
        aa = L.v0.r.e; bb = L.v0.r.s; cc = (u16)L.v0.r.s; m0 = cc + (aa - bb) / 2;
   (fewer pins are not enough: aa alone fixes the load order, bb the register, cc keeps c1's addu in place).  Flagged HACK.
   Untested idea for a natural form: make the c0/shift chain late in sched1 by feeding `hs` into something with a longer dependence path.

8. Tools added (tools/, WSL python3, all parallel with --jobs 3):  vp.py (variants as (old,new) replacement lists, ranked by the aligned metric of vb.py),
   mvs.py (move/swap statement i to every position of a line range; 1600 variants in 70 s for a 185-insn function), asmins.py (insert empty asm("+r")/asm("r")/asm volatile or
   do{}while(0) after each assignment), lamodel.py (model of gcc 2.8.1 local-alloc for one basic block from the sched1 dump: `tools/sv.sh`/dump.sh -> rtlc.py (RTLC_W=5000
   keeps the REG_DEAD notes, the default cuts lines at 230 chars) -> lamodel.py <dir> [--move UID:AFTER_UID]), pr903.py (global-alloc order table), regstat.sh, sv.sh.
   cc1_281 does NOT accept -fsched-verbose (the pmret build has no verbose scheduler log); sched.c/local-alloc.c of the exact compiler are in wip/w/ (fetched from
   gcc-papermario, identical to stock 2.8.1).
9. Shell traps: `$var` inside `wsl -- bash -c '...'` is expanded by the Windows side (empty): put loops into a script file; python3 exists only inside WSL, `python` only on Windows.

==================================================================================================================
ROUND 9 ADDENDUM (s903, second pair of targets: func_0027CCD0 [EXACT], func_002A78F0)
==================================================================================================================
10. func_0027CCD0 (491 insns, EXACT with plain @SN):
   - A `static inline` helper that has its OWN locals (u8 spB8[0x40]; Vec3f spF8;) is what the ROM looks like: every call site re-materialises `addiu aN,sp,off`
     for the locals, whereas the same code written as a macro / inside the one function CSEs the addresses into a saved register.  Use the helper when the ROM
     recomputes `addiu a0,sp,0xB8` before each jal.
   - Copy a vector table entry with a properly TYPED element (`sp28 = cfg->tab->vec[st];` with CcPr vec[14]) - a u8-array/memcpy style copy gives the wrong load/store shape.
   - Two variables with the same type but different life (R / R2) must stay two source variables; merging them changes the register split.
   - A byte field written once (`ob->unk1D1 = D_801041F0.idx;`) must be typed u8 in the struct, otherwise sb becomes sh/sw.
   - y0 (and every temp shared between two sequential code regions) must be declared at FUNCTION scope: it then becomes one multi-set pseudo (not boosted by sched1).

11. func_002A78F0 (410 insns): EXACT with plain @SN, ro=0xCBC60:64 (rodata identical), but it needs 3 HACKs (j, k, g below).  The draft started at 33/102.  Lessons, all verified
    with the compiler (each one moved the aligned metric):
   a) USER VARIABLE SCOPE decides sched1/global-alloc behaviour.  A variable that is assigned in BOTH of two sequential loops (acc, sx, sy, x0, y0, fs0) must be one
      function-level variable: it becomes ONE multi-set pseudo.  Multi-set pseudos are never "birthing" boosted by sched1 (adjust_priority needs REG_N_SETS==1), so
      their final add sinks/floats like the ROM's; and a function-level fs0 lives across the call of loop 1, so global-alloc gives it the callee-saved $f20 in BOTH loops
      (loop-local fs0 = a temp in $f0 -> every other FP register shifts by one).  x0 and y0 behave the same (x0 loop-local made the x0 add the LAST insn before the branch).
   b) The same effect for the colour word: r,g,b are function-level ints set BEFORE the loop (`r = g = b = 0xFF; i = 0; n = i;` then `for (; i < 4; i++)`): that puts
      `li s5,255` first in the preheader (lowest luid) exactly as the ROM, instead of after `i = 0`.
   c) `rgba` partial: `rgba = (r<<24)|(g<<16)|(b<<8); g1->w1 = rgba | ((u32)(fs0*kf) & 0xFF);` (single-set USER variable used in another basic block) reproduces the ROM
      (r<<24|g<<16 hoisted as `lui fp,0xFFFF`, `sll b,8` + `or` at the very end of the block, `or` in the delay slot).  A compiler-temp single expression is a loop movable
      and force_movables chains it into one hoisted constant (0xFFFFFF00) - wrong; a multi-set `rgba |= ...` is never boosted and floats to the block top - wrong.
      Mechanism: loop.c cannot move a SET of a user variable that is used outside its basic block (REG_USERVAR_P) unless the loop is entered unconditionally.
   d) bubble sort compare: `t0 = sl[i]; t1 = sl[i+1]; if ((u32)t0->unk1C > (u32)t1->unk1C)` (NOT `t1 < t0`) gives ROM's lw order and a2/a0 assignment (operand order of the
      compare changes the pseudo creation order and so the global-alloc tie-break).
   e) `n++` placed AFTER the trailing `if (a != s->unk18 && fs0 < k) {..}` statement (end of the body) is what the ROM has: it ends up in the delay slot of the first beq; placed
      right after the call it floats above the call.
   f) Loop-2 preheader `i = 0; n = i;` + `for (; i < 4; i++)` (not `n = 0; for (i = 0; ...)`) gives `addu s1,zero,zero; addu s3,s1,zero` in the ROM order.
   g) HOISTING THE MAGIC CONSTANT of `x / 5` (0x66666667) out of a big loop (ROM: `lui/ori s6` in the preheader).  loop.c move_movables hoists a movable only if
      threshold*savings*lifetime >= insn_count, threshold = (loop has call ? 1 : 2) * (1 + n_non_fixed_regs) = 69 here, minus 3 for every movable already hoisted in that loop;
      savings = number of SETS of the pseudo (not uses), lifetime = luid(REGNO_LAST_UID) - luid(REGNO_FIRST_UID).  A 134-insn loop never hoists a life-1 constant (a tiny
      loop of <= 69 insns does, e.g. a 6-line test).  cse1 DOES extend REGNO_LAST_UID: if a second `/5` with a DIFFERENT dividend sits in the same extended basic block, cse1 replaces
      its K load by the first K register and the first K's life becomes the distance to the second mult (life 9..13) -> hoisted into a callee-saved register.  If the second
      division's result is overwritten before use (`r = (a*0xC1+3)/5; r = 0xFF;`) flow deletes it after the loop pass, so the final code has ONE mult and K lives in
      s6 - exactly the ROM.  Does NOT work: same dividend (fully CSE'd), result never used (jump_optimize deletes it before cse), second division in another EBB (then K
      matches via combine_movables only if both loads survive cse1, savings=2).  So the original source evidently computed a second (dead) /5 value in that arm.
   h) `do { stmt; } while (0);` is NOT a no-op: the NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG/END pair is a scheduling barrier and raises the loop-depth weight of the pseudos inside (global-alloc
      priority) - wrapping the dead-quotient/r,g,b/fs0 group of loop 2's then-arm moved c / &D_80110634 / K into the ROM's s7 / fp / s6 assignment (8/38 -> 6/15; do{}while(0) around the glistp blocks instead is much worse).  Use it as a priority knob
      (like the empty asm("+r") of lesson 2) when the only diff left is a callee-saved permutation.
   i) The R4300 pipeline in mips.md puts ALL FP ops and mult/mfhi in one 'imuldiv' unit (imul SI 5/5, fadd 3/3, fmul SF 5/5, fcmp 1/1, hilo 1/3, load 2, ADJUST_COST makes
      anti/output deps free): backward list scheduling therefore fills the mult/mfhi shadow with any ready ALU insn ("floaters": multi-set sets, constants).  Consequence: the
      position of `li r,255`-style multi-set sets is determined by which stall comes first, and sched2 (after RA) lets them drift further up unless a register false
      dependence pins them (e.g. a pseudo that shares its hard register with the quotient temp).
12. New tools in tools/: ldump.sh (cc1 with -dL -dS -dR ... into a dir), blk.sh (+condensed rtlc txt of sched/sched2/jump/combine/loop), kmov.sh (loop.c decision for the
    0x66666667 movable), ad.py ADFULL=lo-hi env var (full aligned listing of a range), vp.py/mvs.py/asmins.py (see 8).  Source copies in wip/w: loop281.c, mips.md_281,
    mips.h_281, mips.c_281 (from pmret/gcc-papermario master; loop.c is at the repo root, mips files under config/mips/).  WSL /tmp is wiped when the distro idles: keep dumps in ~.
   j) THE LAST 3 OPERAND DIFFS (cvt.s.w result in $f1 instead of fs0's $f20) - GLOBAL vs LOCAL allocno.  A separate temp for `(f32)((a*0xBF)/5+0x40)` is allocated by
      local-alloc ($f1 right after the 1/256 const got $f0).  global.c then calls set_preference(dest=fs0, src=first operand of the mult = the local-alloc'd temp) which gives
      fs0 a preference for $f1, and expand_preferences() merges that preference into every allocno that dies into an insn setting fs0 without conflict (here loop 1's
      `fs0 = f * k01`): loop 1's f flips from $f0 to $f1 (5 operand diffs) and the g / glistp pair of the join block flips a0<->a1 (13 diffs).  The ROM must therefore have had the temp
      as a GLOBAL pseudo (global-alloc runs after set_preference, so no preference arises and the temp simply gets the first free reg above the const).  A pseudo is global when
      it has references in more than one basic block: add a surviving reference in the other arm - `__asm__ volatile("" : "=f"(f));` as first statement after r,g,b = 0x80 in the
      else arm (a non-volatile asm with an unused output is deleted by flow and does not help; a volatile one emits no code).  Do not use a register pin: it gives the same
      perturbation.  (Diagnose with: tools/ldump.sh + tools/pr903.py + the ';; N preferences: M' lines of a.i.greg.)
   k) FLOATER PLACEMENT BETWEEN cvt AND mul.s.  ROM then-arm = mult, sra, mfhi, sra, subu, addiu 64, mtc1, cvt.s.w, li r, move g, lui+lwc1 (1/256), mul.s, j (delay: move b).
      Plain source puts the const load into the mult shadow and mul.s directly behind the cvt.  The recipe that reproduces it: end the quotient/cvt part of the arm in a
      `do { ...; f = (f32)(..); } while (0);`, then `r = 0xFF; g = 0xFF; b = 0xFF;` and `fs0 = f * 0.00390625f;` as separate statements - the LOOP_BEG/LOOP_END note pair splits sched1
      and sched2 into regions, so the multi-set sets (r,g,b) and the const load are scheduled together with the final mul.s after the cvt.  (180 placements of the wrapper, statement
      orders, asm pins and expression forms without the temp could not do it.)
   Final recipe of the then-arm (loop 2):
        do { r = (a * 0xC1 + 3) / 5; f = (f32)((a * 0xBF) / 5 + 0x40); } while (0);   // dead /5 -> K hoist (g), barrier (k)
        r = 0xFF; g = 0xFF; b = 0xFF;   fs0 = f * 0.00390625f;
      else arm:  r = g = b = 0x80;  __asm__ volatile("" : "=f"(f));  fs0 = 0.5f;           // global allocno (j)
    The original source was surely different (the dead /5 and the asm are stand-ins for whatever made those pseudos/blocks in the real code); they emit nothing.

FINDINGS s903 (round 9, @SN toolchain; targets func_0023A4D0 [708 insns] and func_0040BE38 [185 insns]) -- new lessons only
RESULT: both EXACT under @SN (func_0040BE38 needs -fno-builtin and three register pins, see 7).

0. WORKFLOW THAT WORKED (both drafts were old-flag near-misses of earlier workers; re-run them under @SN FIRST: func_0040BE38_sa6 went from "198 insns, 198 words
   off" to 2 words off just by adding `@SN -fno-builtin`; the ROM calls memcpy, @SN without -fno-builtin inlines the 0x50 byte copy).
   Use the ALIGNED metric (structural / operand diff counts) for every step; the positional "N words differ" of chk is meaningless once an insn is shifted.
   func_0023A4D0: n16 draft (16 structural, 7 operand) -> EXACT in ~12 steps, each step below was a 1-line source change.

1. FLOAT POOL = LITERALS.  func_0023A4D0 references each of its 9 pool constants exactly once (768.0 5120.0 10240.0 0.25 0.75 1.0 0.5 16384.0 and the implicit 2^31 of the
   (u32) casts); ROM shows `lui at,%hi(D_800C86A0+4)` style addressing = entries of the function's OWN pool.  Writing them as plain float literals (not `extern f32 D_800C86xx`)
   gives the same code AND chk reports `ro=0xC92A0:36` (whole pool, order and values verified by the new checker + rocheck.py).  The extern version was also code-EXACT but
   its ro= piece was only the 4-byte 2^31 entry.  The pool order is the order of first use in the SOURCE (A0,A4,A8,AC,B0,B4,B8,BC,C0), so keep the expression order.
   A repeated 0.25f (two source uses) is CSE'd into one load/one pool entry, as in the ROM.

2. `asm("" : : "r"(x))` (no output operand) is implicitly VOLATILE: a full scheduling barrier.  The earlier draft used two of them to "fix" the s0/s2/s3 assignment of the three
   switch indices a0/a1/a2, which cost 16 structural diffs elsewhere (hoisted constants and `i = 0` could no longer float to the block top).  What the asm really did was add
   references to a0: global-alloc priority is floor_log2(weighted refs)*refs/live_length, REG_N_REFS is weighted by loop depth (x2 inside the q4 do-while) and a0 had 7 refs vs
   9 for a1/a2 (14 vs 18 weighted: floor_log2 = 3 vs 4).  ONE `__asm__("" : "+r"(a0));` after the switch (non-volatile, an output operand, 2 refs, not a barrier) lifts a0 to 9 refs
   (tie with a1, lower pseudo number wins) and gives the ROM's s0/s2/s3 = a0/a1/a2 with the schedule untouched (30 operand diffs -> 0).  Generic recipe for a pure
   callee-saved permutation: count the refs per pseudo (tools/pr903.py), find the pseudo whose floor_log2 bucket is one too low, add refs with an empty asm("+r").

3. `x = cond ? a : b;` stored to MEMORY (`alp[i] = (i == 0) ? al : 0.0f;`) expands the target address BEFORE the compare and stores each arm directly into it, so cse1 does not
   apply the path knowledge `i == 0` to the address: ROM `swc1 f4,0(t0)` (strength-reduced pointer).  The if/else with two statements `alp[i] = a; ` / `alp[i] = 0.0f;` folds the
   then-arm address to `192(sp)` (constant index).  Use the ternary when the ROM stores through the walking pointer in both arms.
   Also reuse the variable (`al *= unk208 * unk220;` instead of a new a0v) -> `mul.s f4,f4,f0` in place, store from the same FP register.

4. Natural statement order wins over "scheduled" order: write the vector copy as x, y, z (`pts[i].x = v->pos[i].x; pts[i].y = ey; pts[i].z = ...`): the ROM's load of x before the
   store of y comes from the independent store filling the load delay slot; writing y first keeps the load behind the (may-alias) store.

5. LOOP PREHEADER ORDER (unboosted initial-ready insns are emitted in luid order, higher-latency FP loads last): `i = 1;` BEFORE `p0 = &pts[0];` and an explicit FP variable
   `zero = 0.0f;` BEFORE `lim = 1.0f;` (both plain statements before the loop, loop uses `for (; i < 5; i++)` and compares `dd >= zero`) gives the ROM order
   [li i][p0][giv init][mtc1 zero][lwc1 lim]; the implicit hoisted 0.0 constant of loop.c is emitted after the explicit loads.

6. Two loads whose order is wrong although registers are right (lwc1 536(s7) vs 524(s7)): move BOTH loads textually to just after `cls = e->cls;` (tt0 then tt1) and drop them
   from their old places; moving one of them only changes the FP register numbers.  (luid order of boosted loads at the block top = order of the assignments.)

7. func_0040BE38 (os.vi mode editor).  The last 2 words are local-alloc, not scheduling: `lh a1,16(sp)` (ROM) vs `lh v1,16(sp)` (every natural source).  local-alloc facts
   (modelled in tools/lamodel.py, which reproduces the real choices of this block): output ties to the FIRST dying input (a - b ties sub to a, so the three-insn
   expression a/sub/sra chain is ONE quantity in v0); quantities are allocated by floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/(death-birth), ties by birth order, first-fit over regs 2..;
   b gets v1 unless v1 is busy over [lh b, subu].  The ROM has `subu v1,a0,a3` / `addu v1,v1,v0` (hm - hw + xb/2) live across that point, i.e. in the ROM's sched1 output the
   c0 add and the `hs = c0 << 16` shift sit AFTER the halfword loads (sched2 later hoists them to the top; the model confirms b -> a1 when insn 156 is moved after the lh).
   No natural source variant (1600 single statement moves, 96 empty-asm insertions, 56K permuter iterations, many expression forms) reproduces that sched1 order, because the
   shift's pseudo (`hs`, multi-set) is unboosted and floats to the block top.  EXACT form:  three register pins on the temporaries of the first block only
        register s32 bb __asm__("$5"); register s32 aa __asm__("$2"); register u32 cc __asm__("$8");
        aa = L.v0.r.e; bb = L.v0.r.s; cc = (u16)L.v0.r.s; m0 = cc + (aa - bb) / 2;
   (fewer pins are not enough: aa alone fixes the load order, bb the register, cc keeps c1's addu in place).  Flagged HACK.
   Untested idea for a natural form: make the c0/shift chain late in sched1 by feeding `hs` into something with a longer dependence path.

8. Tools added (tools/, WSL python3, all parallel with --jobs 3):  vp.py (variants as (old,new) replacement lists, ranked by the aligned metric of vb.py),
   mvs.py (move/swap statement i to every position of a line range; 1600 variants in 70 s for a 185-insn function), asmins.py (insert empty asm("+r")/asm("r")/asm volatile or
   do{}while(0) after each assignment), lamodel.py (model of gcc 2.8.1 local-alloc for one basic block from the sched1 dump: `tools/sv.sh`/dump.sh -> rtlc.py (RTLC_W=5000
   keeps the REG_DEAD notes, the default cuts lines at 230 chars) -> lamodel.py <dir> [--move UID:AFTER_UID]), pr903.py (global-alloc order table), regstat.sh, sv.sh.
   cc1_281 does NOT accept -fsched-verbose (the pmret build has no verbose scheduler log); sched.c/local-alloc.c of the exact compiler are in wip/w/ (fetched from
   gcc-papermario, identical to stock 2.8.1).
9. Shell traps: `$var` inside `wsl -- bash -c '...'` is expanded by the Windows side (empty): put loops into a script file; python3 exists only inside WSL, `python` only on Windows.

==================================================================================================================
ROUND 9 ADDENDUM (s903, second pair of targets: func_0027CCD0 [EXACT], func_002A78F0)
==================================================================================================================
10. func_0027CCD0 (491 insns, EXACT with plain @SN):
   - A `static inline` helper that has its OWN locals (u8 spB8[0x40]; Vec3f spF8;) is what the ROM looks like: every call site re-materialises `addiu aN,sp,off`
     for the locals, whereas the same code written as a macro / inside the one function CSEs the addresses into a saved register.  Use the helper when the ROM
     recomputes `addiu a0,sp,0xB8` before each jal.
   - Copy a vector table entry with a properly TYPED element (`sp28 = cfg->tab->vec[st];` with CcPr vec[14]) - a u8-array/memcpy style copy gives the wrong load/store shape.
   - Two variables with the same type but different life (R / R2) must stay two source variables; merging them changes the register split.
   - A byte field written once (`ob->unk1D1 = D_801041F0.idx;`) must be typed u8 in the struct, otherwise sb becomes sh/sw.
   - y0 (and every temp shared between two sequential code regions) must be declared at FUNCTION scope: it then becomes one multi-set pseudo (not boosted by sched1).

      else arm:  r = g = b = 0x80;  __asm__ volatile("" : "=f"(f));  fs0 = 0.5f;           // global allocno (j)
    The original source was surely different (the dead /5 and the asm are stand-ins for whatever made those pseudos/blocks in the real code); they emit nothing.

=== ROUND 9b FINDINGS (s901 s903 s904 s905 s906: allocator rules, sched1, preferences, pins) (workspace s904) ===
FINDINGS s904 (round 9, hard leftovers; @SN toolchain) -- new lessons only

RESULT: both targets EXACT with @SN (no -fno-builtin): func_00414480 (563 insns, double formatter, ro=0xE1D18:224) and func_002529E4 (390 insns, debug heap map).
Method that worked for both: take the best earlier draft, read the ALIGNED diff (tools/ad.py via tools/adf.sh) cluster by cluster, test every hypothesis as a source variant
with vb.py (tools/vbx.sh, ~4 s per variant, 3 jobs), look at the compiler dumps (tools/dump.sh + tools/pm.py + wip/sh/lm.sh) to see WHY a cluster differs, and let the
permuter close the last word of an otherwise exact structure.

=========================== func_00414480 (double formatter): 31 structural / 36 operand -> EXACT ===========================
1. SPLIT VARIABLES BY ROLE.  A draft that reuses one C variable for unrelated jobs (n = pow-loop counter AND int-part value, lim = 'g' limit AND pad count, a1p = rounding
   pointer AND output cursor) gets ONE pseudo with a long, high-priority live range and the registers of every temp around it shift.  The ROM had different registers for each
   job (n v1 / iv a0, lim v1 / padn a1, a1p a1 / op a0): giving each job its own variable took the operand diffs 34 -> 24 -> 8 without touching the structure.
   Tool: tools/pm.py <dump dir of tools/dump.sh> prints the global-alloc order with refs / live length / priority / hard reg per pseudo (the pseudo with 47 refs in a0 was the
   output cursor that also served as the rounding pointer).
2. OUTPUT LOOPS AS `for (j = 0; j < n; j++) *op++ = ...;` (not hand-written `if (n > 0) do { } while (j < n)`): same code, but the pseudo priorities of j/op then match the
   ROM (operand diffs 35 -> 8).  Use natural for-loops for fill/copy loops before anything else.
3. LOOP ROTATION: `if (0.0 < d) { while (d < lim2) { d *= 10; e10--; if (!(0.0 < d)) break; } }` (m2c / hand transcription of the rotated loop) compiles as a rotated loop with the
   wrong test duplicated and the invariant constant load (ldc1 10.0) inside the loop.  The ROM = source `while (0.0 < d && d < lim2) { d *= 10.0; e10--; }`: jump.c duplicates
   the FIRST exit test of a while loop (here `0.0 < d`) in front of the loop and at its bottom (as the conditional back branch), the second test stays at the loop top and the
   constant is hoisted into the preheader after the guard.  A manual for(;;)/do-while/goto form of the rotated loop is NOT equivalent (no LOOP_BEG notes -> no hoisting).
   Write the original un-rotated source and let jump.c rotate it.
4. `d == 0.0 -> goto L614` (shared `dof = 1; nint = 0;` block) instead of duplicating `dof = 1` before a goto to the following label: reorg then steals the first insn of L614
   into the slot of a branch-likely (`bc1tl L61C ; li a0,1`) and skips the redundant `nint = 0` at the target (redundant_insn).  The duplicated-assignment form produced
   `bc1f ; nop ; j ; li` (relax_delay_slots does not invert a branch around an unconditional jump whose slot is already filled).
5. WHERE `dof = 0` SITS DECIDES THE ZERO SOURCE REGISTER: with `dof = 0;` after the join of the three pw arms, reload_cse knows a0 == 0 in the following straight-line code and
   rewrites `li s6,0` / `move s4,s6` into `move s6,a0` / `move s4,a0` (it prefers the lowest register holding the value).  The ROM has `addu s6,zero,zero ; addu s4,s6,zero`:
   `dof = 0;` at the END OF EACH ARM (before the join label; reload_cse forgets everything at a label).
6. `sgn = e10 < 0;` (store-flag = `srl a1,s1,31` in the delay slot of `bgez s1`) placed BEFORE `if (e10 < 0) e10 = -e10;`, `u8 sgn`, test `if (sgn != 0) '-' else '+'`
   (orientation decides beqz vs bnez).  The old `carry = 0; if (e10 < 0) { e10 = -e10; carry = 1; }` gives li/move pairs.
7. Loop-preheader copies: `f2 = fr;` must come BEFORE the guard `if (nexp < prec)` and BEFORE `i = 0;` (ROM: mov.d in the bc1f slot and at the join, i = 0 in the guard's
   slot).  Statement order inside a call loop: `f2 = modf(f2 * 10.0, &t1); ndig++; i++; ...` (ndig++ after the call statement lets sched2 put it right before the jal =
   reorg's delay-slot filler; written before the call it lands before the argument setup).  Found with a 20-permutation brute force of the 5 loop statements
   (generator in the transcript, wip/sh/vbfiles.sh ranks a glob of variants).
8. BRANCH-LIKELY FILL (`beql a0,zero,L ; <first insn of L>` vs ours `beqz a0,L ; <2nd insn of the fall-through>`) -- solved by the PERMUTER with an empty
   `do { } while (0);` as the last statement of the then-block before the join label.  Mechanism (reorg.c mostly_true_jump): scanning backwards from the branch target
   label over NOTEs, a NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG makes the branch "very likely taken" (return 2), so fill_eager_delay_slots tries the TARGET thread first and steals its first insn
   with annulment.  Without it an EQ test is predicted not taken, the fall-through thread is owned and its 2nd insn (`li v0,49`) is eligible, which is what we got.
   Recipe: when the ROM has the target's insn in a branch-likely slot, try an empty do-while(0) (or anything that leaves a LOOP_BEG note) right before the join label.
9. Only hack left: `asm("" : : "f"(pw))` after `pw *= 0.1` (pw = $f20, rnd = $f21; without it rnd has the higher allocation priority 4030 vs 3750 and they swap).
10. Native semantic check of a refactored draft (wip/sem/run.sh old.c new.c N): compile both versions with gcc -O0 against the host modf and compare outputs on random
    doubles / prec / width / ch; strip `__asm__` lines first.  Cheap insurance when reshuffling gotos (400000 cases, 0 diffs here).

=========================== func_002529E4 (debug heap map): 8 structural / 61 operand (s703) -> EXACT ===========================
11. THE ROOT CAUSE OF "the ROM keeps (x0&0x3FF)<<14 inside the pass loop" is the loop.c MOVABLE BUDGET: move_movables doubles insn_count (`insn_count *= 2`) for EVERY movable whose
    register was already moved once by the inner loop, cumulatively, whether or not it is finally moved; the test is threshold*savings*lifetime >= insn_count with
    threshold = 2*(1+n_non_fixed_regs) (~124 without a call in the loop) and all hoisted regs showing the same lifetime (~265).  With an outer loop of ~197 insns exactly
    7 movables are moved, the 8th is "not desirable", and a `cond` movable whose `forces` movable was not moved is "not safe" (log: tools/dump.sh + wip/sh/lm.sh prints the
    "Insn N: regno R ... moved/not desirable" lines of every loop pass).  In the ROM A = x0&0x3FF is hoisted out of BOTH loops while S = A<<14 stays after the guard of the pass
    loop, i.e. S is the 8th movable: ONE more movable than ours precedes it.  Proof of concept: a dummy invariant store before the first use pushed A to the 8th place.
    FIX WITHOUT VISIBLE CODE: write `x0 & 0x3FF` as the SHIFT PAIR `((u32)x0 << 22) >> 22` (macro RECTA): at loop.c time it is two movables (ashift, lshiftrt), combine later folds
    the pair into `andi 0x3FF`; also use the pair form in the straight-line top-fill rect so that cse2 merges the hoisted copy with it (ROM `andi v0,s7,0x3ff ; addu s5,v0,zero`).
    Generic recipe: when loop.c hoists one level too far, make the invariant a longer chain of simple ops that combine folds back (shift pairs, add+neg) to burn the doubling budget.
12. CSE SKIP-BLOCK FOLLOWING (-fcse-skip-blocks, on at -O2) folds `b = 0xFF; ...; if (top < bot) {rect} ...; t = (b*3)>>1` to a constant and merges the two `-1` constants on both sides of the
    skip block into one hoistable pseudo; the ROM did NOT follow that block (multiply present, two separate `addiu v0,zero,-1`).  Checked: -fno-cse-skip-blocks turns the natural
    50-structural draft into 12 but the flag is wrong globally (43 of 149 EXACT @SN functions break with it, 4 of 94 in this memory-manager module), a second jump to the end label
    (cse_end_of_basic_block: LABEL_NUSES == 1 is required) does stop the following but leaves visible code, and jumps/labels that disappear before cse2 do not help because cse2
    follows the block again.  Wrappers around the rect (do{}while(0), for(;;){break;}, switch(0), else{;}, goto) change nothing.  What worked: make the highlight blue value opaque with
    an empty asm: a SEPARATE variable `register s32 hb __asm__("$9"); hb = 0xFF; ... __asm__("" : "+r"(hb)); ... (hb*3)>>1` (no copy; a `bb = b; asm("+r"(bb))` copy leaves a `move v1,t1`;
    an asm directly on the shared u32 b makes b lose its nonzero_bits and adds `andi 0xff` masks to the colour code).  The true original construct is still unknown.
13. COLOUR WORD: `(tt < 0x100) ? ((hi | ((tt & 0xFF) << 8)) | 0xFF) : 0xFFFFFFFF` with `hi = 0xFFFF0000` in a VARIABLE (not a literal) keeps the constants out of tree-level reassociation
    (fold turned `0xFFFF0000 | x | 0xFF` into `x | 0xFFFF00FF` = lui+ori) and gives the ROM's `lui a1,0xffff ; or v0,v0,a1 ; ori v0,v0,0xff` (a register constant that cannot be an
    ori immediate stays a register after cse).  Put the whole expression, multiply included, inside the macro argument (statement expression) so that the gfx pointer
    load/increment precedes the multiply as in the ROM; the two arm values then form ONE pseudo set in both arms (not movable: the -1 stays in place).
14. `do { } while (0);` between two macro invocations is a sched1 barrier: without it sched1 hoists the next macro's `lw gfx` above the previous `sw v0,4(a0)` (the scalar global and the
    struct-field store are independent for alias.c), the loaded pointer then overlaps the colour word and the word is allocated v1 instead of v0 (structural diff 2 -> 0).
15. REGISTER-ALLOCATION PRIORITY BY EXTRA REFERENCES: ROM order r($t0) > b($t1) > g($t2) > p($t3) > bot($t4) could not be pinned (a pinned colour channel loses nonzero_bits ->
    `andi 0xff` masks and structural changes) but is reproduced by adding REFERENCES with a volatile empty asm right after the colour selection:
    `__asm__("" : : "r"(r), "r"(r), "r"(r), "r"(b));`  (priority = floor_log2(refs)*refs/live length; 3 extra refs of r lift it above b, 1 extra ref of b above g; the asm does not extend
    the live ranges).  Use pm.py to read the numbers, then add/remove operands.  Operands that appear twice in one asm count twice.
16. Pins and the allocator: a local `register ... asm("$N")` variable keeps $N out of reload's choices for the WHOLE function, not only inside its live range: with r pinned to $t0 the
    pre-loop `mflo` temp moved to $t2 (ROM $t0).  So pin only what must be pinned (here the highlight blue `hb` -> $t1, which is needed because the ROM shares t1 with b; unpinned it
    takes $v1 and drags the gfx+8 temp and the shared w0 word with it), and use extra references (15) for the rest.  A keep-alive `asm("" : : "r"(var))` at the end of a block stops the
    allocator from reusing a pinned register as a temp inside the block (greedy search wip/sh/greedy_pin2.py).
17. Greedy pin / variant tooling written for this round: wip/sh/greedy_pin.py, greedy_pin2.py (pin search with vb.py scoring), tools/vbx.sh (vb.py with file sync), tools/adf.sh (aligned
    diff of a file), tools/sdf.sh (full side by side), tools/pm.py (allocation table), wip/sh/lm.sh (loop.c movable log), wip/sh/pstat.sh / pdiff.sh / pkill_mine.sh / prun_bg.sh (permuter
    runs on a private copy ~/permuter_s904).  All helper scripts of tools/agents hardcode ~/trw_s904_sNNN and /mnt/d/Projects/ws/sNNN: sed them before use (done in tools/).

DEAD ENDS (do not repeat): do{}while(0) wrapped around every gbi macro (phony loops, 101 structural); r/gc/b as variables assigned in the highlight block (register pressure -> extra s8, 25 structural);
a `yy = y1` copy or a statement-expression copy as the extra loop.c movable (cse1 removes the copy before loop.c); explicit per-pass locals xs/xs8 with hand-written rect words (A part of the
x0+8 chain gets hoisted); asm("+r") directly on u32 b; pins on gc or b; permuter from the s703 hack draft (1190 -> 1035 in 47k iterations: the loop.c budget is not reachable by random mutation).

=== ROUND 9b FINDINGS (s901 s903 s904 s905 s906: allocator rules, sched1, preferences, pins) (workspace s905) ===
FINDINGS s905 (round 9, @SN toolchain) - new lessons only

func_00235AD0  EXACT  (view viewport/scissor/fog setup + colour clear, upstream func_80235AE0_de(Racer *); file wip/out/func_00235AD0.c, header include/s905_types.h)
=======================================================================================================================================
How it was found (workflow that worked): 98 -> 15 -> 8 -> 4 -> 0 aligned-diff words in four steps, each step came from READING the GCC 2.8.1 sources
(D:\Projects\ext\gcc281src: sched.c, cse.c, rtlanal.c semantics) and then testing ONE hypothesis on the real cc1 (tools/dd.sh RTL dumps, `-dS` scheduler log via sv.sh,
tools/rr.sh).  The last step was a hill climber over source *shape* (tools/gen.py: spec = statement order + option flags -> source, scored by tools/vb2.py aligned diff);
a plain permuter / single-statement-move hill climber never found it because the winning change needed 4 coordinated edits.

1. display-list pushes: upstream gbi.h macros (`_GBI_CMD(pkt,a,b) { Gfx *_gbi = (Gfx *)(pkt); _gbi->words.w0 = a; _gbi->words.w1 = b; }`), pkt = `D_80110634++`.
   gSPLookAt: `#define gSPLookAt(pkt, la) { gSPLookAtX(pkt, la); gSPLookAtY(pkt, (char *)(la) + 16); }` (single base pointer, +16).
   Custom gSPFogPosition(pkt,min,max) = gSPMoveWord(pkt, G_MW_FOG, 0, (_SHIFTL(128000/((max)-(min)),16,16) | _SHIFTL((500-(min))*256/((max)-(min)),0,16))).
   Pass fields the ROM loads AFTER a command's w0 store directly as macro arguments (`gSPFogPosition(D_80110634++, o->unk53E, 1000)`), not through a pre-loaded temp.
2. MEMORY ALIAS CLASSES DECIDE THE SCHEDULE.  true_dependence/anti_dependence (rtlanal.c): two refs are independent only if one is {MEM_IN_STRUCT_P, varying address} and the
   other is {not in struct, fixed address} (or the offsets/bases are provably disjoint).  `_gbi->words.w0 = ..` and `o->field` are in-struct+varying; so
   - a global that the ROM reads in a different order than the source order of the Gfx stores must be an AGGREGATE (in-struct, fixed): `extern u32 D_801462C8[]; D_801462C8[0]`,
     `extern struct {..} *D_8011FE80[]; fe = D_8011FE80[0];` (placed right after the preceding compare).  A scalar live-out global load is a boosted "birth", sched1 puts it at the
     END of its block and sched2 can then never hoist it above the `sw x,0(s2)` pointer stores (true dependence on the symbol-less register address after RA).
   - locals that the ROM loads/stores freely around Gfx stores (colour bytes at 0x18/0x19/0x1A(sp): the second command's `lui;sw w0` is hoisted ABOVE the lbu of the colour,
     and the fill-colour `lbu` is hoisted above the preceding w0 store) must be NON-struct fixed-address stack scalars, NOT u8 c[3] / structs.  Plain u8 scalars get callee-saved
     registers (they out-prioritise the x/y pseudos in global alloc), a dead `&c` does not help (2.8.1 keeps (addressof (reg)); purge_addressof turns it back into a register unless
     the address escapes or the access is volatile).  Zero-cost fixes: (a) pass the addresses to a `static inline void set3(u8 *a, u8 *b, u8 *c, u8 x, u8 y, u8 z) { *a = x; *b = y; *c = z; }` called once as
     `set3(&c0, &c1, &c2, 0, 0, 0)` (the address escapes into the inline's param pseudos -> put_addressof_into_stack; the stores fold to `sb zero,24(sp)` and the last one still
     fills the `j` delay slot) - this is what the delivered source uses; (b) ONE volatile access per variable, `*(volatile u8 *)&c0 = 0;` (a volatile MEM forces the same), but a
     volatile store is never put into a branch delay slot (reorg), so keep it off the last store before a `j`.  Every other access stays a plain, CSE-able load/store.
3. cse1 invalidation (cse.c invalidate_memory): ANY store through an in-struct varying address (S4 = `o->unk10 = x`, every `_gbi->words.wN = ..`) removes ALL cached in-struct MEM
   expressions.  A field read before and after such a store is therefore TWO loads in the RTL; reload_cse_regs later deletes the second one (same hard reg) or turns it into
   `mov.s fA,fB` (different hard regs, value still in fB).  This is where the ROM's `lwc1 f1,0x110(s1) ; swc1 f1,12(s1) ; mov.s f0,f1` comes from: S3 `o->unkC = o->unk110` followed by
   the READ-BACK `o->unkC` in `vs0 = (s32)(2.0f * w * o->unkC)`, and the same read-back `o->unk10` for vs1 (`vs1 = (s32)(2.0f * o->height * o->unk10)`, the last of the statements).
   The conversion happens only if, in the sched1 output order, the read-back load comes BEFORE any def of the register that holds the stored value (here the o->x load).
4. sched1 (sched.c, backward list scheduler) facts needed for that: priority = latency-weighted depth from the block start; `birthing_insn_p` boost (0x7f000001) is given to an insn
   that becomes ready when its destination is live in the already-scheduled suffix AND REG_N_SETS==1 (so plain single-set loads are placed right before their consumer, user
   variables with 2 sets are not); insns without successors are made predecessors of the block's final branch/call and boosted when that is scheduled; ties: priority, then class
   (data dependent on the last scheduled insn with latency>1 loses), then INSN_LUID (later in the source = scheduled first = placed LATER), then largest potential hazard; the FP
   multiplier blocks the `mul.s` for several cycles (idle slots are filled by the low-priority user-variable copies `x0 = subreg`).  A load that is a SET OF A HARD REGISTER (a
   `register f32 t __asm__("$f1")` temp) is never boosted (REG_N_SETS of a hard reg is 0), so it floats to the TOP of the block like the ROM's width/y/unk110 loads.
   ONE such pin is enough (`register f32 yf __asm__("$f2")` for o->y, or xf=$f1 for o->x; pinning only o->width is NOT enough; the other two loads can be plain `f32` temps; no pin at all
   leaves 11 structural words): the delivered source uses yf only, with the statement order
   [S1 S2 S3 S4 WL YL XL V0 X0 Y0 X1 Y1 W H V1] (S* = the four o->unkC/unk10 stores, WL/YL/XL = the width/y/x temp loads, V0/V1 = the vscale ints, W/H = wm1/hm1; wip/gen/final_spec.json).
   `xf + wf` / `yf + o->height` (x first) so that the add.s result reuses the register of the first operand like the ROM (`add.s f1,f1,f3`, `add.s f2,f2,f0`).
   Pins on loads that the ROM places LATE (unk114, height) are harmful: they float to the top and wreck the schedule.
5. Small things: s16 locals (`s16 y0,x0,x1,y1`) give the `trunc/mfc1 a3; addu s7,a3,zero` copies and sll/sra at every compare; the clamp limits are s16 locals
   `wm1 = D_800E28D0 - 1; hm1 = D_800E28D4 - 1;` assigned before the ifs while the compares keep `D_800E28D0 - 1 < x1`; the viewport scale ints vs0/vs1 are s16 locals computed before
   the clamps; `s0 = func_0029A958() != 2;` gives `xori;sltu`; the three float pool constants 1.0f, 0.25f, 4.0f are literals (2.0f folds to add.s) -> chk ro=0xC8FA8:12.
6. Tooling traps: `(void)&c;` / a dead pointer assignment do NOT make a variable memory resident; the dump `a.i.cse` shows loads that reload_cse_regs deletes later (judge by the final asm);
   the permuter score is not the aligned diff (a hit on the fog arm gave a lower score and no aligned improvement); vb2/ad.py `movs=` counts mov.s in the first 70 insns.

=== ROUND 9b FINDINGS (s901 s903 s904 s905 s906: allocator rules, sched1, preferences, pins) (workspace s906) ===
FINDINGS s906 (round 9) -- new lessons only.  Targets: func_002627C0 (+func_00262ABC), func_00278530, func_0028A924 (EXACT), func_002A78F0 (reassigned to s903, analysed only)

=== A. func_00278530 (EXACT, @SN -fno-builtin): ORDER OF TWO LOOP-PREHEADER COPIES ===
ROM preheader of the inlined clamp loop:  blez t1 ; addiu v0,s3,8 (slot) ; move a0,v0 ; move t0,s6 ; L: move a2,t0 ...
  a0 = loop.c giv init of the pointer giv p = vp + 16*i (vp = b->v already in v0);  t0 = copy of the output pointer `out`.
Plain C ALWAYS gives the opposite order [move t0,s6][move a0,v0]:
  * loop.c emits movables first and giv inits LAST (just before NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG), i.e. after every plain source copy;
  * a plain copy `vq = vp` of the giv base (vq dies in the giv init) is folded away by combine into the giv init (r271 = r226), so it cannot
    be used to put the `a0 = v0` copy first (y1/y2 tests);
  * pins do not help: loop.c treats hard-reg variables as NON-invariant (n_times_set[hard] = 1), so a pinned base kills the pointer giv.
Recipe that gives the exact ROM order (three EMPTY volatile asms in the guarded preheader, no code emitted):
      if (i < n) {
          __asm__ volatile("" : : : "$4");              /* vp is live across it -> vp cannot share a0 with vq */
          vq = vp;                                      /* plain copy of the giv base; loop uses  p = vq + i  */
          __asm__ volatile("" : : : "$2", "$3");        /* vq (block-local, local-alloc) must avoid v0,v1 -> takes a0; volatile asm pins the order */
          o = out;
          __asm__ volatile("" : : "r"(vq));             /* second use of vq: combine cannot fold the copy forward; sched1 would otherwise sink it (birthing boost) behind `o = out` */
          do { p = vq + i; d = o; o += 4; ... i++; } while (i < n);
      }
  Result: vp = v0 (delay slot), `move a0,v0` = the vq copy, `move t0,s6` = o copy, the giv init `a0 = vq` becomes a no-op (g and vq both a0).
  Each asm is needed (w1 without the first one coalesces vp, vq and the walker in a0: 243 insns).  Several equivalent spellings give 0/0 (asm2 may also take "r"(vq); asm3 may add "memory").
  General rule: to put a loop.c giv-init copy BEFORE a plain copy in the preheader, route the giv base through a block-local pseudo vq, keep vq alive past the plain copy
  (asm use), force vq into the walker's register (clobber v0/v1 inside its range) and keep the vp->vq copy from coalescing (clobber the walker's reg while vp is live).
Mechanisms verified in the gcc 2.8.1 sources (D:/Projects/ext/gcc281src):
  * local-alloc picks the FIRST free hard reg (no REG_ALLOC_ORDER on MIPS => $2,$3,$4,...) for block-local pseudos; global.c set_preference() turns a copy with an already
    allocated local pseudo into a copy preference of the global partner => vp follows vq's register unless they conflict.
  * sched1 rank_for_schedule: priority, then dependence class, then INSN_LUID (original order).  A move chain does NOT raise priority (latency 1 => priority stays 1), so
    equal-priority moves keep their IR order; only birthing insns (dest REG_N_SETS==1 and live, released by a scheduled user) are boosted and sink next to their use.
  * Copy order can therefore only be changed in the IR, never by the schedulers.

=== B. func_00278530: INLINED OUT-OF-LINE-DEFERRED `inline` FUNCTION (func_00278A78) ===
  * func_00278A78 has no caller (no jal anywhere) and sits AFTER func_00278530 in the ROM: it is an `inline` function whose out-of-line copy is emitted at the end; the
    loop in func_00278530 is its INLINED body.  Reproduce with `static inline void clampv(R06VBlk *b, u8 *out)` + the MN/MX/CL macros of src/game/func_00278A78.c, called as
    `if (ret != 0) clampv(dst, cp);`.
  * Inline expansion copies EVERY non-const formal through a fresh pseudo at the call site (integrate.c: `! TREE_READONLY (formal)` -> copy_to_mode_reg).  cse1 removes the copy
    again if the callee does not modify the param (the old reg stays canonical); if the callee modifies its param (`out += 4` as in the standalone body) the copy stays at the
    CALL SITE, i.e. in front of `i = 0` and the guard (`move t0,s6` first).  The ROM has the walker copy AFTER the guard, so the callee body used a local copy inside the guard:
    `if (i < n) { o = out; do {...} while (i < n); }` (also gets the loop rotated exactly like the ROM).
  * walker idiom `move a2,t0 ... addiu t0,a2,4` (d = o; o += 4): cse1 makes d canonical (its last use is beyond the EBB and later than o's) so `o = o + 4` becomes `o = d + 4` and o is
    NOT a biv.  If o is made a biv (e.g. a later use of o after the loop) loop.c reduces the three stores as DEST_ADDR givs (`sb v0,-2(a2)`), so the idiom needs the non-iv form.
  * giv `d = o` (mult 1, add 0) has benefit 1; strength_reduce subtracts add_cost (2) => never reduced ("not worth while, -32760 vs 46"); DEST_ADDR givs with mult 1/add 0 are not recorded.
  * Helper must be `static inline`; plain `inline` would add an out-of-line definition (chk rule).

=== C. func_0028A924 (EXACT with the lead's pin) ===
  * Two reads of the same global (`if (D_8011FE94 != 0) ... use(D_8011FE94, D_8011FE94)`) that the ROM does NOT merge (test in v0, arg 1 `move a1,v0`, arg 2 `move a2,a1`):
    `sz = D_8011FE94; if (sz != 0) { sz = 0; ... }` -- the dead write `sz = 0` kills the cse equivalence of the first load; and take the call args from an existing MULTI-SET local
    (sched1 birthing boost needs REG_N_SETS == 1, so a multi-set pseudo's load stays at the top of the block and reload_cse later turns the second load into `move`).
  * The remaining a1/a2 role swap of the doubled argument temp was fixed by a pin on the temp: `{ register s32 av __asm__("$5"); av = D_8011FE94; h = func_002518DC(0, av, av, ...); }` (lead).
    The decomp-permuter (20 min, 3 jobs, base score 20) found nothing.

=== D. func_002627C0 (two functions in 314 insns) ===

    (D:/Projects/ext/ragewars-decomp/src): static inline take_effect/spawn_effect helpers, EXACT @SN with the OLD include/their headers (chk marker `/* their2 */` in the first 3 lines
    selects include/their2).  func_00262ABC needs `extern char D_8011FE88;`, `->unk5F24`, `unk5F00.v0` and `&...unk5F00.v1` spellings.

=== F. TOOLING PITFALLS (this workspace) ===
  * tools/perm_strip.py dropped `static inline` helper definitions (permuter base compiled to 201 insns instead of 244, score 4740).  Use the s804 version (keeps `inline` helpers).
  * Several agent tools wrote fixed /tmp names (ad.py, sbsm.py, sbs.py, side.py, score.py, lst.py, regvote.py): in parallel workspaces they clobber each other; names are per-pid now.
  * `exec(compile(open('tools/lu_match.py')...))` helper scripts (sbsm.py, pre.py) inherit lu_match's globals: a script that uses names like `lo`/`hi` gets them overwritten by
    `for lo, hi, n in REGIONS` -- use unique variable names.
  * `&`-backgrounded permuter jobs die when the wsl call returns; run them with run_in_background and `timeout`.
  * Dump recipe used for all loop/sched questions: `EXTRA='-fno-builtin -fsched-verbose=5' bash tools/dump.sh <file.c> /tmp/dd_x` (a.i.loop = loop.c log + RTL after loop,
    a.i.sched / a.i.sched2 = sched logs with "ready list" priorities), `python3 tools/prio2.py /tmp/dd_x` = global-alloc order/registers per pseudo.

FINDINGS s906 (round 9) -- new lessons only.  Targets: func_002627C0 (+func_00262ABC), func_00278530, func_0028A924 (EXACT), func_002A78F0 (reassigned to s903, analysed only)

=== A. func_00278530 (EXACT, @SN -fno-builtin): ORDER OF TWO LOOP-PREHEADER COPIES ===
ROM preheader of the inlined clamp loop:  blez t1 ; addiu v0,s3,8 (slot) ; move a0,v0 ; move t0,s6 ; L: move a2,t0 ...
  a0 = loop.c giv init of the pointer giv p = vp + 16*i (vp = b->v already in v0);  t0 = copy of the output pointer `out`.
Plain C ALWAYS gives the opposite order [move t0,s6][move a0,v0]:
  * loop.c emits movables first and giv inits LAST (just before NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG), i.e. after every plain source copy;
  * a plain copy `vq = vp` of the giv base (vq dies in the giv init) is folded away by combine into the giv init (r271 = r226), so it cannot
    be used to put the `a0 = v0` copy first (y1/y2 tests);
  * pins do not help: loop.c treats hard-reg variables as NON-invariant (n_times_set[hard] = 1), so a pinned base kills the pointer giv.
Recipe that gives the exact ROM order (three EMPTY volatile asms in the guarded preheader, no code emitted):
      if (i < n) {
          __asm__ volatile("" : : : "$4");              /* vp is live across it -> vp cannot share a0 with vq */
          vq = vp;                                      /* plain copy of the giv base; loop uses  p = vq + i  */
          __asm__ volatile("" : : : "$2", "$3");        /* vq (block-local, local-alloc) must avoid v0,v1 -> takes a0; volatile asm pins the order */
          o = out;
          __asm__ volatile("" : : "r"(vq));             /* second use of vq: combine cannot fold the copy forward; sched1 would otherwise sink it (birthing boost) behind `o = out` */
          do { p = vq + i; d = o; o += 4; ... i++; } while (i < n);
      }
  Result: vp = v0 (delay slot), `move a0,v0` = the vq copy, `move t0,s6` = o copy, the giv init `a0 = vq` becomes a no-op (g and vq both a0).
  Each asm is needed (w1 without the first one coalesces vp, vq and the walker in a0: 243 insns).  Several equivalent spellings give 0/0 (asm2 may also take "r"(vq); asm3 may add "memory").
  General rule: to put a loop.c giv-init copy BEFORE a plain copy in the preheader, route the giv base through a block-local pseudo vq, keep vq alive past the plain copy
  (asm use), force vq into the walker's register (clobber v0/v1 inside its range) and keep the vp->vq copy from coalescing (clobber the walker's reg while vp is live).
Mechanisms verified in the gcc 2.8.1 sources (D:/Projects/ext/gcc281src):
  * local-alloc picks the FIRST free hard reg (no REG_ALLOC_ORDER on MIPS => $2,$3,$4,...) for block-local pseudos; global.c set_preference() turns a copy with an already
    allocated local pseudo into a copy preference of the global partner => vp follows vq's register unless they conflict.
  * sched1 rank_for_schedule: priority, then dependence class, then INSN_LUID (original order).  A move chain does NOT raise priority (latency 1 => priority stays 1), so
    equal-priority moves keep their IR order; only birthing insns (dest REG_N_SETS==1 and live, released by a scheduled user) are boosted and sink next to their use.
  * Copy order can therefore only be changed in the IR, never by the schedulers.

=== B. func_00278530: INLINED OUT-OF-LINE-DEFERRED `inline` FUNCTION (func_00278A78) ===
  * func_00278A78 has no caller (no jal anywhere) and sits AFTER func_00278530 in the ROM: it is an `inline` function whose out-of-line copy is emitted at the end; the
    loop in func_00278530 is its INLINED body.  Reproduce with `static inline void clampv(R06VBlk *b, u8 *out)` + the MN/MX/CL macros of src/game/func_00278A78.c, called as
    `if (ret != 0) clampv(dst, cp);`.
  * Inline expansion copies EVERY non-const formal through a fresh pseudo at the call site (integrate.c: `! TREE_READONLY (formal)` -> copy_to_mode_reg).  cse1 removes the copy
    again if the callee does not modify the param (the old reg stays canonical); if the callee modifies its param (`out += 4` as in the standalone body) the copy stays at the
    CALL SITE, i.e. in front of `i = 0` and the guard (`move t0,s6` first).  The ROM has the walker copy AFTER the guard, so the callee body used a local copy inside the guard:
    `if (i < n) { o = out; do {...} while (i < n); }` (also gets the loop rotated exactly like the ROM).
  * walker idiom `move a2,t0 ... addiu t0,a2,4` (d = o; o += 4): cse1 makes d canonical (its last use is beyond the EBB and later than o's) so `o = o + 4` becomes `o = d + 4` and o is
    NOT a biv.  If o is made a biv (e.g. a later use of o after the loop) loop.c reduces the three stores as DEST_ADDR givs (`sb v0,-2(a2)`), so the idiom needs the non-iv form.
  * giv `d = o` (mult 1, add 0) has benefit 1; strength_reduce subtracts add_cost (2) => never reduced ("not worth while, -32760 vs 46"); DEST_ADDR givs with mult 1/add 0 are not recorded.
  * Helper must be `static inline`; plain `inline` would add an out-of-line definition (chk rule).

=== C. func_0028A924 (EXACT with the lead's pin) ===
  * Two reads of the same global (`if (D_8011FE94 != 0) ... use(D_8011FE94, D_8011FE94)`) that the ROM does NOT merge (test in v0, arg 1 `move a1,v0`, arg 2 `move a2,a1`):
    `sz = D_8011FE94; if (sz != 0) { sz = 0; ... }` -- the dead write `sz = 0` kills the cse equivalence of the first load; and take the call args from an existing MULTI-SET local
    (sched1 birthing boost needs REG_N_SETS == 1, so a multi-set pseudo's load stays at the top of the block and reload_cse later turns the second load into `move`).
  * The remaining a1/a2 role swap of the doubled argument temp was fixed by a pin on the temp: `{ register s32 av __asm__("$5"); av = D_8011FE94; h = func_002518DC(0, av, av, ...); }` (lead).
    The decomp-permuter (20 min, 3 jobs, base score 20) found nothing.

    (D:/Projects/ext/ragewars-decomp/src): static inline take_effect/spawn_effect helpers, EXACT @SN with the OLD include/their headers (chk marker `/* their2 */` in the first 3 lines
    selects include/their2).  func_00262ABC needs `extern char D_8011FE88;`, `->unk5F24`, `unk5F00.v0` and `&...unk5F00.v1` spellings.

=== ROUND 9c FINDINGS (s902: func_00418224 loop.c hoisting vs pins, parameter preferences, SDK shift order) (workspace s902) ===
ROUND 9 / s902 FINDINGS (new lessons only)
==========================================

1. func_00403658 (EXACT): a float LITERAL stored into a frame local of a `static inline` helper keeps the inline-frame pseudo alive.
   integrate.c gives the inlined callee frame a pseudo (force_reg(loc), const_equiv = loc) and try_constants/subst_constants substitute
   it into every copied insn only if apply_change_group accepts the WHOLE insn.  A store whose source is a register with a known constant
   value (a float literal 10.0f from the pool) becomes `(set (mem frame) (const_double))` after substitution = unrecognisable = the whole
   substitution of that insn is rejected, so the pseudo survives and the ROM keeps `addiu s0,sp,80` + P-relative stores/`move a0,s0`.
   With `extern f32 D_800E0CA8` (a memory source) everything folds to sp-relative addressing and the draft is 4 words off.
   => when the ROM keeps a frame pointer pseudo in an inlined struct-returning helper, check whether a constant is stored into the
      helper's local: it must be a LITERAL in the source (this also fixes the pool order), never an extern.
   `do { res.z = 0; } while (0)` (loop notes = scheduling barrier) was needed for the `move v0,s1` placement.

2. func_00290A68 (EXACT, needs 2 hacks): how a DEAD load ends up above `addiu sp,sp,-N` (sched2 mechanics, verified with
   -fsched-verbose=5 / tools/sverb.sh and cc1 `-fno-delayed-branch` to see the pre-reorg order).
   a) sched2 (reload_completed) is a REVERSE list scheduler: it picks from the end of the block.  Prologue stores (sw ra/s0..sN) are
      in block 0 (only if the first block does not start with a label), all of them ready right after the last call/branch.
      Pick rules (schedule_select): highest priority group first (priority = longest dependence chain from the block start, load edges
      cost 2, everything else 1 -> almost always all 1); inside the group a load that follows a STORE pick is blocked for 1 cycle; among
      the unblocked the largest potential hazard wins (store > load > ALU); ties by luid (later insn first).
   b) Consequence: a load that is ready from the beginning of the pre-call phase is picked at the first free ALU slot (right after
      the `li a1` of the call), i.e. it lands next to the argument moves.  It reaches the TOP of the block (above addiu sp) only if it is
      released at the same moment as a store: the stores `sw s3,..` / `sw s4,..` of the prologue are released when the insn that writes
      s3 / s4 (the `la s3,sym` / `move s4,a0`) is picked; if the load's LAST dependent is that same insn, the store preempts it and the
      load is blocked in the next cycle; the alternation store/ALU/store/ALU then starves the load to the very top.
      A dead load has no dependents, so it needs an artificial consumer: edges to an asm are FREE (INSN_CODE < 0 -> cost 1),
      edges to a recognised insn cost 2 (the producer is queued 2 cycles).
   c) The working source: `extern volatile s32 D_801536B8;` (the read must survive flow) + a NON-volatile asm that consumes the loaded
      value and is the writer of the callee-saved register:  p = (f32 *)D_801536B8; __asm__("la %0,D_8011FE88" : "=r"(tb) : "r"(p));
      (tb pinned `register u8 *tb __asm__("$19")` only fixes the s2/s3 order of tb vs the -1 constant).  An EMPTY template with
      "=r"(tb) : "0"(D_8011FE88), "r"(p) does NOT work: the compiler emits a separate `la` before the asm, the load and the `la` are
      released together and the load wins the next slot.  A volatile asm is a full barrier (depends on every earlier insn incl. all
      prologue saves) and puts the argument move after the saves - the opposite of the ROM.
      The plain `tb = D_8011FE88;` before the asm is dead on the MIPS build (flow deletes it) and keeps a TARGET_PC build valid.
   d) Things that look related but are NOT the cause: a dead if/else on the same volatile (jump2 deletes branch + feeder with
      delete_computation, but the diamond splits the basic block so the argument move `move a0,s3` ends up in the join block,
      AFTER the four register saves); `do { } while (0)` (LOOP notes make the first insn after them depend on EVERY earlier insn,
      saves included); an orphan volatile read alone (lands after `move a0,s3`).
   e) Other sched facts found on the way: no-op moves (`move a2,a2`) are turned into notes at the START of sched2 (they do not take
      part in it); sched1 dependence links (LOG_LINKS) stay on the insns and are reused by sched2 (stale edges, e.g. OUTPUT edge between
      two sets of the same pseudo); a call is a barrier only for call-used hard registers (a load into v1 can not move below it);
      jump_optimize delete_computation removes a dead branch's feeder only if SET_DEST is pointer-equal to the REG_DEAD note's reg and
      only walking back over plain INSNs.
   f) Tools: wip/sh/noslot.sh <file> [n] shows the pre-reorg (sched2) order via -fno-delayed-branch; tools/sverb.sh <file> -dR sched2 the
      pick log; `bash tools/rs.sh greg` the RTL with the dependence lists of the insns; wip/sh/sdv.sh <n> files... side-by-side of variants.

3. func_00418224 (EXACT, no pins, no asm): a 767-insn display-list loop.  What got it from 287/292 (aligned structural/operand) to 0/0:
   a) loop.c NEVER hoists anything computed from a hard register (scan_loop: `for i < FIRST_PSEUDO_REGISTER: may_not_optimize[i] = 1, n_times_set[i] = 1`).
      My register pins on tw / tsz (the SDK macro inputs) therefore killed the ROM structure: in the ROM the pre-loop block is loop.c output (constants,
      &D_80110634, the F540 line word, (tw-1)*4 tile-size word, tw*stripRows, (tsz+3)>>2, (tsz+1)>>1, stripRows-1) and the sp20/sp24/sp28/sp2C stack slots are
      those hoisted temporaries SPILLED (slot offsets follow pseudo-creation order).  Write the invariant SDK-macro arguments INLINE in the loop body
      (#define'd expressions, no variables) and let loop.c hoist; a plain pre-loop `sp24 = ...;` statement gives a different block order / pseudo numbers.
      Only pin values that do not feed invariants.  The loop dump (`-dL`, wip/sh/... `Loop from..: Insn N: regno R (life L), savings S moved/not desirable`)
      tells which constants/insns were hoisted (savings >= 3..4 moves a constant; identical insns "match" and add up).
   b) Parameters that the ROM modifies in place (`y1 <<= 10`) must stay the PARAMETER pseudos.  The incoming-register move gives the pseudo a hard register
      preference (y1 -> a3, x1 -> a2); global-alloc find_reg pass 0 makes every HIGHER-priority conflicting pseudo avoid the registers preferred by LOWER-priority
      conflicting ones (prune_preferences/regs_someone_prefers), unless that register is in the preferring pseudo's own hard_reg_conflicts.  With `Y1 = y1` copied
      into a pinned local the preference disappears and the whole low-register assignment shifts by one (hoisted &D_80110634 took a3 instead of t0, tw t1 instead
      of t2, img pointer/0x07080200 swapped a2/a3): that was the entire remaining 52-operand diff.  Tools: regdump.py (conflicts, order, hard reg per pseudo),
      wip/sh/prio2.py (global-alloc order table: refs, live length, priority guess, hard reg), wip/sh/rdump.sh <file> <pseudo...>.
   c) Copies feeding later float conversions: `hCopy = h;` BEFORE the first use of h (cse then uses the copy in the first conversion too and reload_cse rewrites
      the register back: the ROM's `addu a2,a0,zero` right before `mtc1 a0`), `wCopy = w;` likewise; both give the ROM's a3/a2 live ranges (a2/a3 are free after
      the param moves).  Placement after the first use leaves the copy at the end of the block (wrong register, wrong place).
   d) Anything that must be computed first (here `(s32)srcH` for the zero test, before `(s32)(srcH * K)`) goes into its own variable assigned earlier:
      `srcHi = (s32)srcH;` orders the two trunc.w.s/mfc1 like the ROM (sched1 keeps source order for equal-latency insns).
   e) The SDK `_SHIFTL(G_TEXRECT,24,8) | _SHIFTL(lrx,12,12) | _SHIFTL(lry,0,12)` order must be kept in the source: `(0xE4000000 | lrx_part) | lry_part`
      reproduces the ROM tail (constant `lui a0,0xe400` early, `or v0,v0,a0` on the lry part first); `E4 | (lrx | lry)` schedules differently and that changes
      which low registers the local pseudos of the tail use, which in turn changes every global allocation (local-alloc registers are hard-reg conflicts of globals).
   f) switch cases that store a table value and divide by the same loaded value: `stripRows = (K / D_800E32A4->w) & ~1;` BEFORE `rowBytes = ...` keeps the loaded
      value in a register (the other order spills it: store + reload before the div).
   g) Dead ends worth knowing: volatile locals instead of spills (much worse), named pinned constants instead of loop.c hoisting (wrong order), pins on the loop
      temporaries (t3/t4/t7/img: no gain once the allocation is right), clobber asms on $6 (made things worse).
   Method for big functions: aligned listing (wip/sh/aln.py <file> <fn> <rom> [lo hi]), windowed score (wip/sh/vbw.py --win lo hi), per-window scan (winscan.sh),
   generators for variants (wip/sh/gen_*.py) + vbrun.sh to rank them; fix the first window with structural diffs, then the next.

4. General: `wip/sh/noslot.sh <file.c> [n]` = cc1 with -fno-delayed-branch (the order sched2 produced before reorg filled delay slots);
   a call's argument moves (`move a0,s3`, `li a1,..`) belong to the same basic block as the call: a (dead) branch before the call puts them
   in the block AFTER the prologue saves, a straight line keeps them before the saves (the ROM's case).

      into a pinned local the preference disappears and the whole low-register assignment shifts by one (hoisted &D_80110634 took a3 instead of t0, tw t1 instead
      of t2, img pointer/0x07080200 swapped a2/a3): that was the entire remaining 52-operand diff.  Tools: regdump.py (conflicts, order, hard reg per pseudo),
      wip/sh/prio2.py (global-alloc order table: refs, live length, priority guess, hard reg), wip/sh/rdump.sh <file> <pseudo...>.
   c) Copies feeding later float conversions: `hCopy = h;` BEFORE the first use of h (cse then uses the copy in the first conversion too and reload_cse rewrites
      the register back: the ROM's `addu a2,a0,zero` right before `mtc1 a0`), `wCopy = w;` likewise; both give the ROM's a3/a2 live ranges (a2/a3 are free after
      the param moves).  Placement after the first use leaves the copy at the end of the block (wrong register, wrong place).
   d) Anything that must be computed first (here `(s32)srcH` for the zero test, before `(s32)(srcH * K)`) goes into its own variable assigned earlier:
      `srcHi = (s32)srcH;` orders the two trunc.w.s/mfc1 like the ROM (sched1 keeps source order for equal-latency insns).
   e) The SDK `_SHIFTL(G_TEXRECT,24,8) | _SHIFTL(lrx,12,12) | _SHIFTL(lry,0,12)` order must be kept in the source: `(0xE4000000 | lrx_part) | lry_part`
      reproduces the ROM tail (constant `lui a0,0xe400` early, `or v0,v0,a0` on the lry part first); `E4 | (lrx | lry)` schedules differently and that changes
      which low registers the local pseudos of the tail use, which in turn changes every global allocation (local-alloc registers are hard-reg conflicts of globals).
   f) switch cases that store a table value and divide by the same loaded value: `stripRows = (K / D_800E32A4->w) & ~1;` BEFORE `rowBytes = ...` keeps the loaded
      value in a register (the other order spills it: store + reload before the div).
   g) Dead ends worth knowing: volatile locals instead of spills (much worse), named pinned constants instead of loop.c hoisting (wrong order), pins on the loop
      temporaries (t3/t4/t7/img: no gain once the allocation is right), clobber asms on $6 (made things worse).
   Method for big functions: aligned listing (wip/sh/aln.py <file> <fn> <rom> [lo hi]), windowed score (wip/sh/vbw.py --win lo hi), per-window scan (winscan.sh),
   generators for variants (wip/sh/gen_*.py) + vbrun.sh to rank them; fix the first window with structural diffs, then the next.

4. General: `wip/sh/noslot.sh <file.c> [n]` = cc1 with -fno-delayed-branch (the order sched2 produced before reorg filled delay slots);
   a call's argument moves (`move a0,s3`, `li a1,..`) belong to the same basic block as the call: a (dead) branch before the call puts them
   in the block AFTER the prologue saves, a straight line keeps them before the saves (the ROM's case).
